# List 3

## Unit 1

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>barb</b></td><td>/bɑːrb/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>尖锐而严厉的批评</td><td>a sharp barb<br>尖刻的讥讽</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her final barb about his cooking silenced the whole table.  她最后那句关于他厨艺的尖刻讥讽让整桌人都不说话了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affront</td><td>/əˈfrʌnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>侮辱，冒犯</td><td>an affront to dignity<br>对尊严的侮辱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His careless remark was an affront to everyone in the room.  他随口的一句话侮辱了房间里的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 criticism</td><td>/ˈkrɪtɪˌsɪzəm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>批评，指责；评论</td><td>harsh criticism<br>严厉的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plan drew harsh criticism from residents at the meeting.  该计划在会上受到居民的严厉批评。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 offense</td><td>/əˈfens/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>冒犯，得罪；犯罪</td><td>take offense at<br>因……而生气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took offense at his joke about her accent.  她因他拿她的口音开玩笑而生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outrage</td><td>/ˈaʊˌtreɪdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>愤怒；暴行；激怒</td><td>public outrage<br>公众的愤怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The verdict caused outrage among the victim's family and friends.  判决引起受害者家人和朋友的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sarcasm</td><td>/ˈsɑːrkæzəm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>讽刺，轻蔑</td><td>a hint of sarcasm<br>一丝讽刺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a faint note of sarcasm in his polite reply.  他礼貌的回答中带着一丝讽刺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 praise</td><td>/ˈpreɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>赞扬，称赞</td><td>win high praise<br>获得高度赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won high praise from serious critics.  她的第一部小说赢得了严肃评论家的高度赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 applause</td><td>/əˈplɔːz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>鼓掌；认可，赞许</td><td>a round of applause<br>一阵掌声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her speech was greeted with long and warm applause.  她的演讲赢得了长时间热烈的掌声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 compliment</td><td>/ˈkɑːmplɪmənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞；恭维</td><td>pay someone a compliment<br>赞美某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He paid her a warm compliment on her new painting.  他对她的新画作说了一句诚挚的赞美。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>barbarous</b></td><td>/ˈbɑːrbərəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>野蛮的，凶残的</td><td>barbarous cruelty<br>野蛮的残忍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The invaders were remembered for their barbarous treatment of prisoners.  侵略者因对俘虏的野蛮对待而被人记住。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brutal</td><td>/ˈbruːtəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>残忍的；直白无情的</td><td>a brutal attack<br>残忍的袭击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoners described the brutal treatment they received in the camp.  囚犯们描述了他们在营中受到的残忍对待。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 atrocious</td><td>/əˈtroʊʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极坏的，极其残忍的</td><td>an atrocious crime<br>残暴的罪行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court described the killing as an atrocious crime.  法庭称这起杀人事件是极其残忍的罪行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fiendish</td><td>/ˈfiːndɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶魔般的，恶毒的；极难的</td><td>a fiendish plot<br>恶毒的阴谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The detective uncovered a fiendish plan to poison the water supply.  侦探揭露了一个恶毒的投毒供水计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heartless</td><td>/ˈhɑːrtləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无情的，狠心的</td><td>a heartless decision<br>无情的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was heartless to fire her the week before Christmas.  在圣诞节前一周解雇她真是无情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 savage</td><td>/ˈsævədʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>凶残的，野蛮的；猛烈攻击</td><td>a savage beating<br>野蛮的殴打</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics savaged the film for its careless treatment of history.  批评家猛烈批评该片对历史的草率处理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 truculent</td><td>/ˈtrʌkjələnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好战的，好斗的；粗野的</td><td>a truculent response<br>咄咄逼人的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The truculent teenager argued with every teacher who tried to correct him.  这个好斗的少年跟每位试图纠正他的老师都吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vicious</td><td>/ˈvɪʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>凶恶的，恶毒的</td><td>a vicious circle<br>恶性循环</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog gave a vicious bite that required several stitches.  那只狗凶恶地咬了一口，需要缝几针。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 merciful</td><td>/ˈmɜːrsɪfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>仁慈的，宽厚的</td><td>a merciful judge<br>宽仁的法官</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court was merciful and gave him only a small fine.  法庭很宽仁，只罚了他少许款项。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 benevolent</td><td>/bəˈnevələnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>仁慈的，行善的</td><td>a benevolent employer<br>仁慈的雇主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A benevolent stranger paid for the family's train tickets.  一位仁慈的陌生人为这家人买了火车票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humane</td><td>/hjuˈmeɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>人道的，仁慈的</td><td>humane treatment of prisoners<br>人道地对待囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Activists demanded more humane conditions inside the detention center.  活动人士要求改善拘留中心内的人道条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sympathetic</td><td>/ˌsɪmpəˈθetɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>同情的，有同情心的</td><td>a sympathetic listener<br>富有同情心的倾听者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse gave her a sympathetic look and held her hand.  护士同情地看着她，握住了她的手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>barefaced</b></td><td>/ˈberfeɪst/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>公然的，厚颜无耻的</td><td>a barefaced lie<br>明目张胆的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He told a barefaced lie to the committee without blinking.  他毫不眨眼地对委员会撒了个明目张胆的谎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apparent</td><td>/əˈperənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的；表面上的</td><td>for no apparent reason<br>没有明显原因</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It soon became apparent that the machine had never worked.  很快就明显看出这台机器从未运转过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plain</td><td>/ˈpleɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>完全的，十足的；朴素的，清楚的</td><td>plain stupid<br>简直愚蠢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ignoring the warning was just plain stupid.  忽视警告纯粹是愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bald</td><td>/ˈbɔːld/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>秃头的；不加掩饰的</td><td>the bald truth<br>赤裸裸的事实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave us the bald facts without any comfort or explanation.  他直白地告知事实，没有任何安慰或解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evident</td><td>/ˈevɪdənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，显然的</td><td>clearly evident<br>显而易见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her talent for music was evident from a very early age.  她的音乐才华从很小就显而易见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 manifest</td><td>/ˈmænɪfest/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>显然的，明显易懂的；显现，显露</td><td>manifest injustice<br>明显的不公</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His discomfort at the question was manifest to everyone seated around the table.  他对这个问题的不适让围坐桌边的每个人都看得明明白白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obvious</td><td>/ˈɑːbviəs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，显而易见的</td><td>an obvious mistake<br>明显的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The answer seemed obvious once she explained the method.  她解释了方法后，答案似乎显而易见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 perspicuous</del></td><td>/pərˈspɪkjuəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>表述清晰的，明白易懂的</td><td>a perspicuous style<br>明白易懂的文风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His writing is perspicuous even when the topic is technical.  即使话题很专业，他的文章也清晰易懂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 secret</td><td>/ˈsiːkrət/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>秘密的，隐秘的；秘密</td><td>keep a secret<br>保守秘密</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They held secret meetings in the basement for several months.  他们在地下室秘密开会数月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 furtive</td><td>/ˈfɜːrtɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>鬼鬼祟祟的；秘密的</td><td>a furtive glance<br>偷偷的一瞥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He cast a furtive glance at the answers on her desk.  他偷偷瞥了一眼她桌上的答案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clandestine</td><td>/klænˈdestɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>秘密的，暗中的</td><td>a clandestine meeting<br>秘密的会面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They arranged a clandestine meeting in an empty parking garage.  他们在一个空停车场安排了秘密会面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 surreptitious</td><td>/ˌsɜːrəpˈtɪʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>偷偷摸摸的，保密的</td><td>a surreptitious glance<br>偷偷的一瞥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He took a surreptitious glance at his watch during the meeting.  会上他偷偷看了一眼手表。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bargain</b></td><td>/ˈbɑːrɡən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>协议；讨价还价</td><td>drive a hard bargain<br>狠狠地讨价还价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dealer likes to drive a hard bargain with new customers.  这个商人喜欢和新客户狠狠地讨价还价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accord</td><td>/əˈkɔːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>一致，符合；授予</td><td>in accord with<br>与…一致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His actions were in accord with the policy he announced.  他的行动与他宣布的政策一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compact</td><td>/ˈkɑːmpækt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>契约，协议；紧凑的</td><td>sign a compact<br>签订协议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two nations signed a compact to protect the shared river.  两国签订协议保护共有的河流。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contract</td><td>/ˈkɑːnˌtrækt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>契约，合同；收缩；感染疾病</td><td>sign a contract<br>签订合同</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both parties signed the contract without reading the final clause carefully.  双方都没有仔细读最后一条就签了合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 covenant</td><td>/ˈkʌvənənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>契约，盟约</td><td>a solemn covenant<br>庄严的盟约</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two families entered a covenant to share the land.  两家订立盟约共用这块土地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deal</td><td>/ˈdiːl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>交易，协议；处理</td><td>close a deal<br>达成交易</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They finally struck a deal after weeks of tense talks.  经过数周紧张谈判，他们终于达成交易。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pact</td><td>/ˈpækt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>协定，条约</td><td>sign a peace pact<br>签订和平协定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The neighbors made a pact never to sell to developers.  邻居们订下协定，绝不卖给开发商。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 settlement</td><td>/ˈsetəlmənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>和解，协议；定居点</td><td>reach a settlement<br>达成和解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company reached a settlement with the injured workers last month.  公司上月与受伤工人达成和解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 haggle</td><td>/ˈhæɡəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>讨价还价，争论不休</td><td>haggle over the price<br>就价格讨价还价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tourists haggle with vendors over the price of every souvenir.  游客与摊贩为每件纪念品讨价还价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 negotiate</td><td>/nɪˈɡoʊʃieɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>商量，谈判</td><td>negotiate a contract<br>谈判一份合同</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two sides negotiated a contract that satisfied neither of them completely.  双方谈成了一份合同，但双方都不完全满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>baroque</b></td><td>/bəˈroʊk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>装饰华丽的，过分雕琢的，复杂的</td><td>baroque ornamentation<br>华丽繁复的装饰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ceiling was covered with heavy baroque ornamentation in gold.  天花板上覆满了华丽繁复的金色装饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 byzantine</td><td>/ˈbɪzəntiːn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>错综复杂的</td><td>byzantine bureaucracy<br>错综复杂的官僚体制</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tax code is so byzantine that even experts disagree.  税法错综复杂，连专家都各执一词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 complicated</td><td>/ˈkɑːmplɪkeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>复杂的，难懂的</td><td>a complicated process<br>复杂的流程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tax rules are so complicated that most people hire help.  税务规定如此复杂，多数人都请人帮忙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 convoluted</td><td>/ˈkɑːnvəluːtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>复杂的，费解的</td><td>a convoluted argument<br>费解的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The instructions were so convoluted that nobody could assemble the shelf.  说明书太过费解，没人能装好那个架子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elaborate</td><td>/ɪˈlæbəreɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>详细阐述；精心制作的</td><td>elaborate on a point<br>详述某一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Could you elaborate on your last point a little?  你能就最后一点再详细说明一下吗？</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fancy</td><td>/ˈfænsi/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>喜爱，想要</td><td>fancy a cup of tea<br>想喝杯茶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do you fancy a walk along the river this evening?  今晚想不想去河边散散步？</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intricate</td><td>/ˈɪntrəkət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>错综复杂的，精细的</td><td>an intricate pattern<br>精细的图案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The carpet shows an intricate pattern of leaves and birds.  地毯上是精细复杂的叶与鸟图案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 knotty</td><td>/ˈnɑːti/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>复杂的，困难的</td><td>a knotty question<br>棘手的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor posed a knotty question that nobody could answer confidently.  教授提出了一个没人能自信回答的棘手问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 labyrinthine</td><td>/ˌlæbəˈrɪnθaɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷宫似的，复杂曲折的</td><td>labyrinthine corridors<br>迷宫般的走廊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We got lost in the labyrinthine corridors of the old hospital building.  我们在这座老医院大楼迷宫般的走廊里迷了路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lavish</td><td>/ˈlævɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>奢侈的，大量的，无节制的；挥霍，浪费</td><td>a lavish banquet<br>奢华的宴会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They hosted a lavish banquet for three hundred guests in the garden.  他们在花园里为三百位客人举办了一场奢华的宴会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tangled</td><td>/ˈtæŋɡəld/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>纠缠的，错综复杂的</td><td>a tangled web<br>错综复杂的关系网</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The case involves a tangled web of loans and false names.  此案涉及贷款与假名交织的复杂网络。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 austere</td><td>/ɔːˈstɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>朴素的，朴实无华的</td><td>an austere room<br>简朴的房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He slept in an austere room with a bed and one chair.  他睡在一间只有一张床和一把椅子的朴素房间里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plain</td><td>/ˈpleɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>完全的，十足的；朴素的，清楚的</td><td>plain stupid<br>简直愚蠢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ignoring the warning was just plain stupid.  忽视警告纯粹是愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 simple</td><td>/ˈsɪmpəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简单的，容易的</td><td>a simple task<br>简单的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Follow these simple steps and the machine will restart.  按这些简单步骤操作，机器就会重启。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>barrage</b></td><td>/bəˈrɑːʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>有压倒之势的、集中的倾泻（如言语）</td><td>a barrage of questions<br>一连串的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister faced a barrage of questions about the missing funds.  部长面对了关于失踪资金的一连串问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 salvo</td><td>/ˈsælvoʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>齐射；（言语的）猛攻</td><td>an opening salvo<br>开场的猛烈攻击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His article was the first salvo in a long public debate.  他的文章是一场长期公开辩论的第一波攻击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hail</td><td>/ˈheɪl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>冰雹；一阵（如子弹）</td><td>a hail of criticism<br>一阵猛烈的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soldiers advanced through a hail of bullets and smoke.  士兵们在弹雨与烟尘中前进。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cannonade</td><td>/ˌkænəˈneɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>连续炮击，猛烈轰击</td><td>a heavy cannonade<br>猛烈的炮击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The town endured a cannonade that lasted the entire night.  小镇承受了持续整夜的炮击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shower</td><td>/ˈʃaʊər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>阵雨；一阵；淋浴</td><td>a shower of praise<br>一阵赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The winner received a shower of flowers from the excited crowd.  获胜者收到兴奋人群抛来的一阵鲜花。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 storm</td><td>/ˈstɔːrm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>暴风雨；一阵猛烈（如抗议）</td><td>a storm of protest<br>一阵抗议风暴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The decision caused a storm of protest on social media.  这一决定在社交媒体上引发抗议风暴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 volley</td><td>/ˈvɑːli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>齐射；一连串（如问题）</td><td>a volley of questions<br>一连串的提问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister faced a volley of questions from angry reporters.  部长面对愤怒记者的一连串提问。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>barren</b></td><td>/ˈbærən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；无成果的</td><td>barren land<br>贫瘠的土地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing grows on the barren land beyond the last village.  最后一个村子之外的贫瘠土地上什么都长不出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 bootless</del></td><td>/ˈbuːtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无用的</td><td>a bootless effort<br>无用的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Arguing with him about money proved a bootless effort.  和他争论钱的事结果是无用的努力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ineffective</td><td>/ˌɪnɪˈfektɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无效的，不起作用的</td><td>an ineffective treatment<br>无效的治疗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old medicine proved completely ineffective against the new virus.  这种旧药对新病毒完全无效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 inefficacious</del></td><td>/ˌɪnefɪˈkeɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无效的，不能奏效的</td><td>an inefficacious remedy<br>不奏效的疗法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors abandoned the inefficacious drug after two failed trials.  两次试验失败后，医生放弃了这种无效药物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unavailing</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈveɪlɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>徒劳的，无果的</td><td>unavailing efforts<br>徒劳的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 All their efforts to save the old theater proved entirely unavailing.  他们为挽救老剧院所做的一切努力都完全徒劳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vain</td><td>/ˈveɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>徒劳的；自负的</td><td>in vain<br>徒劳地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They searched all night in vain for the missing dog.  他们整夜寻找失踪的狗，却是徒劳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 desolate</td><td>/ˈdesələt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>荒凉的；孤苦凄凉的</td><td>a desolate landscape<br>荒凉的景色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The abandoned village looked desolate under the grey winter sky.  废弃的村庄在灰暗冬空下显得荒凉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impoverished</td><td>/ɪmˈpɑːvərɪʃt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫困的；贫瘠的</td><td>an impoverished region<br>贫困地区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 War left the once-prosperous city utterly impoverished for many years.  战争让这座曾繁荣的城市多年极度贫困。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 waste</td><td>/ˈweɪst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词、动词</td><td>荒芜的；浪费；废物</td><td>waste land<br>荒地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing grows on this waste ground beside the old factory.  旧工厂旁的这片荒地上什么也长不出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 effective</td><td>/ɪˈfektɪv/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有效的，起作用的</td><td>an effective solution<br>有效的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Simple hand washing is an effective way to prevent illness.  简单洗手是预防疾病的有效方法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 successful</td><td>/səkˈsesfəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>成功的，有成效的</td><td>a successful career<br>成功的事业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her second attempt at the exam was finally successful.  她第二次考试终于成功了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 productive</td><td>/prəˈdʌktɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>多产的，富有成效的</td><td>a productive meeting<br>富有成效的会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The morning was productive; we finished three chapters before lunch.  上午很有成效，午饭前我们写完了三章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 virtuous</td><td>/ˈvɜːrtʃuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>品德高尚的，正直的</td><td>a virtuous life<br>高尚的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He led a quiet, virtuous life and helped his neighbors freely.  他过着平静高尚的生活，乐于帮助邻里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 arable</td><td>/ˈærəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>适合耕种的</td><td>arable land<br>可耕种的土地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a narrow strip of arable land lies between the desert and the sea.  沙漠与海之间只有一条狭窄的可耕地带。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fruitful</td><td>/ˈfruːtfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>富有成果的，多产的</td><td>a fruitful discussion<br>富有成果的讨论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their collaboration proved fruitful and produced several important papers.  他们的合作富有成果，产出了几篇重要论文。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 luxuriant</td><td>/lʌɡˈʒʊriənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>茂盛的，繁茂的</td><td>luxuriant vegetation<br>茂盛的植被</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Luxuriant ferns covered the damp floor of the forest.  茂盛的蕨类覆盖了潮湿的林地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 verdant</td><td>/ˈvɜːrdnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（因长满植物而）翠绿的，郁郁葱葱的</td><td>verdant hills<br>翠绿的山丘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After the rains the verdant valley filled with wildflowers.  雨后，翠绿的山谷开满了野花。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>barricade</b></td><td>/ˌbæriˈkeɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>障碍物；用障碍物阻止通过</td><td>build a barricade<br>筑起路障</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students built a barricade of desks across the main gate.  学生们用课桌在大门前筑起路障。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fence</td><td>/ˈfens/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>栅栏，篱笆；围起</td><td>a wooden fence<br>木栅栏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They painted the wooden fence around the garden last summer.  去年夏天他们给花园周围的木栅栏刷了漆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hedge</td><td>/hedʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>树篱，篱笆；用篱笆围住</td><td>a neatly trimmed hedge<br>修剪整齐的树篱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A neatly trimmed hedge separated the garden from the road.  一道修剪整齐的树篱把花园与马路隔开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wall</td><td>/ˈwɔːl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>墙，壁；用墙围住</td><td>a brick wall<br>砖墙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Photographs of his children covered the wall behind the desk.  书桌后的墙上贴满了他孩子的照片。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 barrier</td><td>/ˈbæriər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>障碍，屏障</td><td>a language barrier<br>语言障碍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cost remains the biggest barrier to buying an electric car.  成本仍是购买电动车的最大障碍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obstacle</td><td>/ˈɑːbstəkəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>障碍，阻碍物</td><td>overcome an obstacle<br>克服障碍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lack of funding was the main obstacle to finishing the bridge.  资金不足是完成大桥的主要障碍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blockade</td><td>/ˌblɑːˈkeɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>封锁，阻塞</td><td>impose a blockade<br>实施封锁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The navy set up a blockade around the island's main port.  海军对该岛主港实施了封锁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obstruction</td><td>/əbˈstrʌkʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>障碍物；阻挠</td><td>an obstruction in the road<br>路上的障碍物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police removed the obstruction that had blocked traffic for hours.  警方清除了堵塞交通数小时的障碍物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bar</td><td>/ˈbɑːr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>阻挡，禁止；条，棒；酒吧</td><td>bar the entrance<br>堵住入口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A fallen tree barred the narrow path up the hill.  一棵倒下的树堵住了上山的窄路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 check</td><td>/tʃek/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使突然停止，阻止；检查，核对</td><td>check the spread of disease<br>阻止疾病蔓延</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Strict quarantine measures checked the spread of the disease.  严格的隔离措施阻止了疾病的蔓延。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hinder</td><td>/ˈhɪndər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻碍，妨碍</td><td>hinder progress<br>妨碍进展</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Poor lighting hindered the search for survivors.  照明不足妨碍了对生还者的搜寻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impede</td><td>/ɪmˈpiːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>妨碍，阻碍</td><td>impede progress<br>阻碍进展</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick fog impeded the rescue team's progress.  浓雾阻碍了救援队的前进。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obstruct</td><td>/əbˈstrʌkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>妨碍，阻塞</td><td>obstruct justice<br>妨碍司法公正</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A fallen tree obstructed the only road out of town.  一棵倒下的树堵住了唯一的出城道路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wall off</td><td>/wɔːl ˈɔːf/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>用墙隔开，隔离</td><td>wall off the area<br>把该区域隔开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Builders walled off the damaged wing until repairs could begin.  建筑工人把受损的侧翼隔开，等待开始修复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 permit</td><td>/pərˈmɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>允许，准许；许可证</td><td>permit access<br>允许进入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The school does not permit phones during classroom hours.  学校不允许上课时间使用手机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>barter</b></td><td>/ˈbɑːrtər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>以物换物</td><td>barter goods for food<br>以货物换食物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Farmers used to barter grain for tools and cloth.  农民过去用粮食换取工具和布匹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 swap</td><td>/ˈswɑːp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>交换，调换</td><td>swap seats<br>交换座位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boys swapped stickers during the break between classes.  男孩们在课间交换贴纸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trade</td><td>/ˈtreɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>贸易；交换，交易</td><td>trade with China<br>与中国进行贸易</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two towns traded salt and wool for many centuries.  这两个镇几个世纪以来用盐交换羊毛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>beguile</b></td><td>/bɪˈɡaɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，诱骗；使着迷</td><td>beguile the audience<br>令观众着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magician could beguile an audience for a full hour.  这位魔术师能让观众着迷整整一小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deceive</td><td>/dɪˈsiːv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，误导</td><td>deceive the public<br>欺骗公众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He deceived his investors with promises of quick and easy profits.  他用轻松快速获利的承诺欺骗了投资者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bluff</td><td>/blʌf/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>虚张声势地骗人，唬人</td><td>bluff your way through<br>靠虚张声势混过去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to bluff his way through the whole interview.  他试图靠虚张声势混过整场面试。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 con</td><td>/ˈkɑːn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>欺骗，骗取；骗局</td><td>con artist<br>骗子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swindler conned elderly victims out of their life savings.  那个骗子骗走了老年受害者的毕生积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cozen</td><td>/ˈkʌzn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>诱骗</td><td>cozen him into agreeing<br>诱骗他同意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swindler cozened elderly widows out of their modest life savings.  那个骗子诱骗年老的寡妇们交出微薄的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delude</td><td>/dɪˈluːd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，使误信</td><td>delude oneself<br>自欺欺人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not delude yourself into thinking the danger has passed.  别欺骗自己，以为危险已经过去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dupe</td><td>/duːp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>dupe trusting investors<br>欺骗轻信的投资者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scheme duped trusting investors out of nearly a million dollars.  这个骗局欺骗轻信的投资者，骗走近百万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gull</td><td>/ɡʌl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>gull the unwary<br>欺骗粗心的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swindler gulled dozens of elderly investors out of their savings.  骗子欺骗了数十位老年投资者，骗走他们的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hoodwink</td><td>/ˈhʊdwɪŋk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>hoodwink the voters<br>欺骗选民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company hoodwinked customers with promises it could never possibly keep.  这家公司用根本无法兑现的承诺欺骗顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snooker</td><td>/ˈsnʊkər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，诱骗</td><td>snooker into a deal<br>诱骗做成交易</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They snookered the old man into signing a worthless contract.  他们诱骗那位老人签了一份毫无价值的合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trick</td><td>/ˈtrɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>欺骗，诱骗；诡计</td><td>trick someone into paying<br>诱骗某人付钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She tricked him into revealing the password to the account.  她骗他说出了账户密码。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 undeceive</del></td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈsiːv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使醒悟，使不再受骗</td><td>undeceive the public<br>使公众醒悟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone must undeceive her before she invests everything in that scheme.  必须有人让她醒悟，别把全部钱投进那个计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 2

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>bathetic</b></del></td><td>/bəˈθetɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>故作伤感的；突降的</td><td>a bathetic ending<br>故作伤感的结局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel ends with a bathetic scene of sudden tears.  小说以一个突然落泪、故作伤感的场面收尾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trite</td><td>/traɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，陈词滥调的</td><td>a trite observation<br>陈腐的见解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech relied on trite phrases that moved nobody in the hall.  那篇演讲满是陈腐的套话，没能感动会场里的任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cliché</del></td><td>/kliːˈʃeɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调，老套的说法</td><td>an overused cliché<br>用滥的陈词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Saying that time heals everything has become a tired cliché.  说时间治愈一切已成为老套的陈词滥调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commonplace</td><td>/ˈkɑːmənpleɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平凡的；老生常谈</td><td>a commonplace observation<br>老生常谈的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Video calls have become commonplace in ordinary office life.  视频通话在日常办公中已很平常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hackneyed</td><td>/ˈhæknid/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，缺乏创新的</td><td>a hackneyed phrase<br>陈腐的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech relied on hackneyed images of struggle and triumph.  这篇演讲依赖于陈腐的奋斗与胜利意象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stale</td><td>/steɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈旧乏味的</td><td>stale jokes<br>老掉牙的笑话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His stale jokes made the audience shift restlessly in their seats.  他老掉牙的笑话让观众在座位上不安地挪动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stereotyped</td><td>/ˈsteriətaɪpt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>老套的，刻板的</td><td>stereotyped characters<br>刻板的人物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel is full of stereotyped characters and predictable plots.  这部小说充满刻板人物和可预测的情节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 offbeat</td><td>/ˌɔːfˈbiːt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不平常的</td><td>an offbeat sense of humor<br>别致的幽默感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His offbeat sense of humor won over an audience that had arrived skeptical.  他别致的幽默感赢得了原本抱着怀疑到场的观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>befuddle</b></del></td><td>/bɪˈfʌdl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使困惑</td><td>befuddle the audience<br>使听众困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long tables of statistics only befuddle the general audience.  长长的统计表格只会使普通听众困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confuse</td><td>/kənˈfjuːz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑；混淆</td><td>confuse the issue<br>混淆问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vague instructions confused nearly everyone in the training class.  含糊的说明让培训班几乎所有人都困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 baffle</td><td>/ˈbæfl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使疑惑</td><td>baffle the experts<br>使专家困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strange symptoms continued to baffle the visiting experts.  这些奇怪的症状一直使来访的专家疑惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bewilder</td><td>/bɪˈwɪldər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使迷惑，使不知所措</td><td>bewilder the newcomers<br>使新来者困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The maze of corridors bewildered every visitor on the first day.  迷宫般的走廊让每位来访者第一天都不知所措。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confound</td><td>/kənˈfaʊnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑；证明为假，证伪</td><td>confound the critics<br>让批评者困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The late victory confounded the critics who had predicted failure.  最后的胜利让预言失败的批评者困惑不已。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disorient</td><td>/dɪsˈɔːrient/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使迷失方向，使困惑</td><td>disorient the visitors<br>使来访者迷向</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thick fog disoriented the hikers within a few minutes.  浓雾几分钟内就让徒步者失去了方向感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 flummox</del></td><td>/ˈflʌməks/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑，难住</td><td>completely flummox him<br>把他彻底难住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The final question flummoxed even the best students in class.  最后一题连班上最好的学生都难住了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 muddle</td><td>/ˈmʌdəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使混乱，弄糟；混乱</td><td>muddle the facts<br>把事实弄乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too many footnotes muddled the argument of his short essay.  过多脚注把他短文的论证弄乱了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perplex</td><td>/pərˈpleks/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑，使费解</td><td>perplex the researchers<br>使研究者困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strange results perplexed the researchers for several long months.  奇怪的结果让研究者困惑了好几个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 puzzle</td><td>/ˈpʌzəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使困惑；谜题</td><td>puzzle the doctors<br>使医生们困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sudden recovery puzzled the doctors who had treated her.  她的突然康复让治疗过她的医生们感到困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assure</td><td>/əˈʃʊr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使确信，向…保证</td><td>assure him of support<br>向他保证支持</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager assured us that the missing files were safe.  经理向我们保证，丢失的文件很安全。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reassure</td><td>/ˌriːəˈʃʊr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使安心，打消疑虑</td><td>reassure worried parents<br>安抚焦虑的父母</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The principal tried to reassure worried parents after the accident.  事故之后，校长努力安抚焦虑的父母。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 satisfy</td><td>/ˈsætɪsfaɪ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使满意；使确信</td><td>satisfy the skeptics<br>让怀疑者信服</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The extra evidence satisfied the skeptics who had doubted his story.  额外的证据让怀疑他说法的人信服了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>bedeck</b></del></td><td>/bɪˈdek/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>装饰，点缀</td><td>bedeck the hall with flowers<br>用花装饰大厅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers bedeck the hall with flowers before every summer festival.  每年夏季节庆前，村民都用花装饰大厅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adorn</td><td>/əˈdɔːrn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>装饰，点缀</td><td>adorn the walls<br>装饰墙面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old maps and photographs adorn the walls of the study.  旧地图和照片装饰着书房的墙壁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beautify</td><td>/ˈbjuːtɪˌfaɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>美化，装饰</td><td>beautify the city<br>美化城市</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Volunteers planted trees to beautify the neglected riverside park.  志愿者种树来美化那座荒废的河滨公园。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decorate</td><td>/ˈdekəreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>装饰，布置；授勋</td><td>decorate the room<br>装饰房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children helped decorate the room with paper lanterns.  孩子们帮忙用纸灯笼装饰房间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dress</td><td>/ˈdres/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>装饰，布置；穿衣</td><td>dress the shop window<br>布置商店橱窗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dressed the shop window with silk scarves and dried flowers.  她用丝巾和干花布置了商店橱窗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 embellish</td><td>/ɪmˈbelɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>装饰，修饰；添枝加叶</td><td>embellish a story<br>给故事添枝加叶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He embellished the tale with details that never actually happened.  他给故事添加了从未发生过的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 emblaze</del></td><td>/ɪmˈbleɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>装饰；使光彩夺目</td><td>emblaze the shield<br>装饰那面盾牌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Golden letters emblaze the cover of the ancient prayer book.  金色字母把那本古老祈祷书的封面装饰得璀璨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 strip</td><td>/strɪp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>剥去，剥光（装饰等）</td><td>strip the walls bare<br>把墙剥得光秃</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Workers stripped the walls bare before applying the fresh coat of paint.  工人把墙剥得光秃，然后刷上新漆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>belabor</b></td><td>/bɪˈleɪbər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>过分强调；痛打</td><td>belabor the point<br>反复纠缠一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is no need to belabor the point any further.  没有必要再对这一点过分强调下去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beat</td><td>/ˈbiːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>打，击；击败</td><td>beat him mercilessly<br>无情地打他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guards beat the prisoner until the officer finally intervened.  卫兵殴打囚犯，直到那位军官出面干预。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 baste</td><td>/ˈbeɪst/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>痛打；严厉批评</td><td>baste him with criticism<br>用批评痛击他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The columnist basted the mayor for wasting so much public money.  专栏作家痛批市长浪费了大量公款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 batter</td><td>/ˈbætər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>连续猛击，殴打</td><td>batter down the door<br>砸开那扇门</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters battered down the locked door to reach the child.  消防员砸开锁住的门去救那个孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 excoriate</td><td>/ɪkˈskɔːrieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉批评；擦伤皮肤</td><td>excoriate the government<br>严厉批评政府</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics excoriated the film for its casual cruelty.  批评家严厉批评该片的随意残忍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lambaste</td><td>/læmˈbeɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉斥责，痛骂</td><td>lambaste the government<br>痛斥政府</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lambasted the film for its shallow, sentimental ending.  批评家严厉斥责这部电影结局浅薄而煽情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abuse</td><td>/əˈbjuːz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>辱骂，抨击；不正当或不合理使用</td><td>abuse of power<br>滥用权力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was accused of abusing his position for profit.  他被指控滥用职位谋利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assail</td><td>/əˈseɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抨击，严厉批评</td><td>assail a policy<br>抨击某项政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Opponents assailed the plan as reckless and unfair.  反对者抨击该计划轻率且不公。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 vituperate</del></td><td>/vaɪˈtuːpəreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>谩骂，责骂</td><td>vituperate an opponent<br>谩骂对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The columnist vituperated the mayor in every weekly article.  这位专栏作家在每周的文章里都痛骂市长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 scathe</del></td><td>/skeɪð/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉批评；伤害</td><td>scathe with bitter sarcasm<br>用讽刺严厉批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reviewers scathed the film for its lazy writing and dull acting.  评论者严厉批评这部电影剧本敷衍、表演乏味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dwell on</td><td>/dwel ˈɑːn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>老想着，反复谈论</td><td>dwell on past mistakes<br>反复纠结旧错</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't dwell on the failure; start planning your next attempt instead.  别老想着失败，去筹划下一次尝试吧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harp on</td><td>/hɑːrp ˈɑːn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>喋喋不休地说</td><td>harp on the same issue<br>老提同一问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He kept harping on my old error throughout the whole meeting.  整场会议他都在喋喋不休地说我以前的失误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 applaud</td><td>/əˈplɔːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>称赞，赞成；鼓掌</td><td>applaud the decision<br>赞成这一决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teachers applauded the decision to reduce homework for young pupils.  老师们赞成给低年级学生减少作业的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extol</td><td>/ɪkˈstoʊl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美；吹捧</td><td>extol his virtues<br>赞美他的品德</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors still extol his kindness during the long winter flood.  邻居们至今仍赞美他在漫长冬季洪水期间的善举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 eulogize</td><td>/ˈjuːlədʒaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>称赞；颂扬</td><td>eulogize the late leader<br>颂扬已故领袖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Colleagues gathered to eulogize the scientist who had trained them all.  同事们聚在一起颂扬这位培养了他们所有人的科学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 endorse</td><td>/ɪnˈdɔːrs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>公开支持</td><td>endorse a candidate<br>公开支持某候选人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The union voted to endorse the mayor for a second term.  工会投票支持市长连任。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 acclaim</td><td>/əˈkleɪm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>称赞</td><td>win critical acclaim<br>赢得评论界赞誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won wide critical acclaim across Europe and Asia.  她的第一部小说在欧洲和亚洲赢得了广泛的评论界赞誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disregard</td><td>/ˌdɪsrɪˈɡɑːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>漠视，不关注；缺乏兴趣，缺乏关心</td><td>total disregard for safety<br>完全不顾安全</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their total disregard for safety rules eventually caused a serious accident.  他们完全不顾安全规定，最终造成了一起严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 omit</td><td>/oʊˈmɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>省略，遗漏，忽略</td><td>omit the details<br>省略细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The summary omits the details that made his argument convincing.  摘要省略了使他论证有说服力的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ignore</td><td>/ˌɪɡˈnɔːr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忽视，不理</td><td>ignore the warning<br>忽视警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The captain ignored the warning and sailed into the storm.  船长忽视了警告，驶入了风暴之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 neglect</td><td>/nəˈɡlekt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>忽视，疏忽；忽略，怠慢</td><td>neglect his duties<br>疏忽职责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He neglected his duties for weeks and was finally dismissed.  他数周疏忽职责，最终被解雇了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 slight</td><td>/slaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>轻微的，不重要的；轻视，怠慢</td><td>slight her contribution<br>轻视她的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt slighted when nobody mentioned his contribution during the ceremony.  典礼上无人提及他的贡献，他觉得受到了轻视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 slur over</td><td>/slɜːr ˈoʊvər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>含糊带过，轻描淡写</td><td>slur over the problem<br>含糊带过问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report slurs over the failures and highlights only minor successes.  报告含糊带过失败，只突出一些小成绩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>beleaguer</b></del></td><td>/bɪˈliːɡər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困扰，使烦恼，使消沉</td><td>beleaguer the government<br>困扰政府</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rising prices continued to beleaguer the new government all year.  物价上涨全年不断困扰着新政府。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 annoy</td><td>/əˈnɔɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒，不断烦扰</td><td>annoy the neighbors<br>不断打扰邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The barking dog began to annoy every neighbor on the quiet street.  那只不停犬吠的狗开始烦扰这条安静街上的每位邻居。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harass</td><td>/həˈræs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>烦扰</td><td>harass the witness<br>骚扰证人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Enemy cavalry harassed the retreating army for three long days.  敌方骑兵对撤退的军队骚扰了漫长的三天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pester</td><td>/ˈpestər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>不断打扰，纠缠</td><td>pester him with questions<br>不断问他问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reporters pestered her with questions outside the courthouse.  记者们在法院外不停向她追问。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plague</td><td>/pleɪɡ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>瘟疫；折磨，使…痛苦</td><td>plagued by doubts<br>被疑虑折磨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doubts about the decision plagued him for the rest of the year.  对那个决定的疑虑折磨了他余下的一整年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tease</td><td>/ˈtiːz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>取笑，戏弄；逗弄</td><td>tease his little sister<br>戏弄他的妹妹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boys teased their little sister about her new haircut.  男孩们拿妹妹的新发型取笑她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 delight</td><td>/dɪˈlaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使高兴，使愉快；愉悦</td><td>delight the audience<br>使观众高兴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young pianist delighted the audience with a lively encore.  这位年轻钢琴家用一首活泼的加演曲取悦了观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>belie</b></td><td>/bɪˈlaɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>与…相对立；与…相矛盾</td><td>belie his claims<br>与他的说法矛盾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company's own records belie his claims about the accident.  公司自己的记录与他关于事故的说法相矛盾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conceal</td><td>/kənˈsiːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>隐藏，隐瞒</td><td>conceal the truth<br>隐瞒真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness tried to conceal the truth from the police.  证人试图向警方隐瞒真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curtain</td><td>/ˈkɜːrtən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>遮蔽，掩盖；窗帘</td><td>curtain off the room<br>用帘子隔开房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy drapes curtained the windows and hid the room from view.  厚重的帷幔遮住窗户，把房间藏了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disguise</td><td>/dɪsˈɡaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>伪装，掩饰；伪装</td><td>disguise his intentions<br>掩饰他的意图</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He disguised his disappointment behind a polite and steady smile.  他用礼貌平静的微笑掩饰了失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mask</td><td>/ˈmæsk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>掩盖，掩饰；面具</td><td>mask his anger<br>掩饰他的怒气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loud music masked the sound of the approaching helicopter.  响亮的音乐掩盖了直升机靠近的声音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contradict</td><td>/ˌkɑːntrəˈdɪkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认，反驳；与…相矛盾</td><td>contradict the evidence<br>与证据相矛盾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness's second statement flatly contradicted everything she had said earlier.  证人的第二份陈述与她此前所说完全矛盾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contravene</td><td>/ˌkɑːntrəˈviːn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>违反，反对</td><td>contravene the rules<br>违反规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Building a factory here would contravene several local environmental regulations.  在此建厂会违反数条当地环保法规。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disclose</td><td>/dɪsˈkloʊz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露，公开，透露</td><td>disclose the details<br>公开细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company refused to disclose the details of the settlement.  公司拒绝公开和解协议的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 expose</td><td>/ɪkˈspoʊz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭露；使暴露</td><td>expose the fraud<br>揭露欺诈行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two journalists exposed the fraud after months of quiet investigation.  两名记者经过数月暗中调查揭露了这起欺诈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 agree</td><td>/əˈɡriː/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>同意，赞同；一致</td><td>agree to the terms<br>同意这些条款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both sides finally agreed to the new safety rules.  双方最终同意了新的安全规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bellwether</b></td><td>/ˈbelweðər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>领导者，带头人</td><td>a bellwether of change<br>变化的风向标</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This small town has long been a bellwether of national opinion.  这个小镇长期以来是全国民意的风向标。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 leader</td><td>/ˈliːdər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>领导者，带头人</td><td>an industry leader<br>行业领军者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She became the leader of the movement while still a student.  她还是学生时就成了这场运动的领导者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pacemaker</td><td>/ˈpeɪsmeɪkər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>领跑者，带头者；起搏器</td><td>the pacemaker of reform<br>改革的带头者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The small firm became the pacemaker for the entire software industry.  这家小公司成了整个软件行业的领跑者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pilot</td><td>/ˈpaɪlət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>试验性的，初步的；试行</td><td>a pilot program<br>试点项目</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The school will pilot the new reading program in three classes.  学校将在三个班级试行这个新的阅读项目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trendsetter</td><td>/ˈtrendsetər/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>引领潮流者，开风气者</td><td>a fashion trendsetter<br>时尚潮流引领者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That designer was a trendsetter long before critics noticed her.  那位设计师早在评论界注意到她之前就已引领潮流。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 follower</td><td>/ˈfɑːloʊər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>追随者，拥护者</td><td>a loyal follower<br>忠实的追随者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained a devoted follower of his teacher for thirty years.  他三十年来一直是老师忠诚的追随者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disciple</td><td>/dɪˈsaɪpl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>信徒，门徒，追随者</td><td>a devoted disciple<br>忠诚的信徒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He became a devoted disciple of the old master painter.  他成了这位老画家的忠实追随者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 imitator</td><td>/ˈɪmɪteɪtər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>模仿者，仿效者</td><td>a pale imitator<br>拙劣的模仿者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painter attracted many imitators but never a genuine rival.  这位画家吸引了许多模仿者，却没有真正的对手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>beneficent</b></td><td>/bɪˈnefɪsnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（对个人或社会）有益的</td><td>a beneficent influence<br>有益的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her long service had a beneficent influence on the whole village.  她多年的服务对整个村子产生了有益的影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 kind</td><td>/ˈkaɪnd/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>仁慈的，友好的；种类</td><td>a kind word<br>一句善意的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was kind of her to visit the sick neighbor daily.  她每天去看望生病的邻居，真是好心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 altruistic</td><td>/ˌæltruˈɪstɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无私的，利他的</td><td>altruistic motives<br>利他的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her altruistic work in the clinic earned wide public respect.  她在诊所无私的工作赢得了公众广泛尊敬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 benevolent</td><td>/bəˈnevələnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>仁慈的，行善的</td><td>a benevolent employer<br>仁慈的雇主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A benevolent stranger paid for the family's train tickets.  一位仁慈的陌生人为这家人买了火车票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 philanthropic</td><td>/ˌfɪlənˈθrɑːpɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>博爱的，为他人着想的</td><td>philanthropic donations<br>慈善捐赠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Philanthropic donations paid for the new hospital wing for sick children.  慈善捐赠支付了收治病童的医院新翼楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 benign</td><td>/bɪˈnaɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的</td><td>a benign influence<br>有益无害的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The change in policy proved benign for most small farmers.  事实证明，政策变化对大多数小农户是无害的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compassionate</td><td>/kəmˈpæʃənət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有同情心的，慈悲的</td><td>compassionate care<br>充满关怀的照护</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A compassionate nurse sat with the frightened child all night.  一位有同情心的护士整夜陪着那个受惊的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sympathetic</td><td>/ˌsɪmpəˈθetɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>同情的，有同情心的</td><td>a sympathetic listener<br>富有同情心的倾听者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse gave her a sympathetic look and held her hand.  护士同情地看着她，握住了她的手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 helpful</td><td>/ˈhelpfəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有帮助的；乐于助人的</td><td>a helpful suggestion<br>有益的建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Your notes were extremely helpful when I revised the report.  我修改报告时你的笔记非常有帮助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 advantageous</td><td>/ˌædvənˈteɪdʒəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有利的，有益的</td><td>an advantageous position<br>有利的位置</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new tax rules proved advantageous to small local businesses.  新税收规定对本地小企业很有利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 favorable</td><td>/ˈfeɪvərəbl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有利的</td><td>favorable conditions<br>有利的条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Favorable weather allowed the harvest to finish a week early.  有利的天气让收割提前一周完成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 kindly</td><td>/ˈkaɪndli/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>和善的；亲切地</td><td>a kindly old man<br>一位和善的老人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A kindly neighbor carried her groceries up the steep stairs.  一位和善的邻居帮她把杂货搬上陡楼梯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 profitable</td><td>/ˈprɑːfətəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有利可图的；有益的</td><td>a profitable business<br>赚钱的生意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bakery finally became profitable in its third busy year.  这家面包店在忙碌的第三年终于开始赚钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 salutary</td><td>/ˈsæljəteri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有益健康的；有利的，利好的</td><td>a salutary lesson<br>有益的教训</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The failed launch had a salutary effect on the careless habits of the team.  那次发射失败对团队的马虎习惯起了有益的作用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 atrocious</td><td>/əˈtroʊʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极坏的，极其残忍的</td><td>an atrocious crime<br>残暴的罪行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court described the killing as an atrocious crime.  法庭称这起杀人事件是极其残忍的罪行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 barbarous</td><td>/ˈbɑːrbərəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>野蛮的，凶残的</td><td>barbarous cruelty<br>野蛮的残忍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The invaders were remembered for their barbarous treatment of prisoners.  侵略者因对俘虏的野蛮对待而被人记住。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 truculent</td><td>/ˈtrʌkjələnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好战的，好斗的；粗野的</td><td>a truculent response<br>咄咄逼人的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The truculent teenager argued with every teacher who tried to correct him.  这个好斗的少年跟每位试图纠正他的老师都吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vicious</td><td>/ˈvɪʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>凶恶的，恶毒的</td><td>a vicious circle<br>恶性循环</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog gave a vicious bite that required several stitches.  那只狗凶恶地咬了一口，需要缝几针。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 detrimental</td><td>/ˌdetrəˈmentəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的，不利的</td><td>detrimental to your health<br>对你的健康有害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too much screen time is detrimental to a child's eyesight.  过多看屏幕对孩子的视力有害。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 harmful</td><td>/ˈhɑːrmfəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的，造成伤害的</td><td>harmful to health<br>对健康有害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too much screen time can be harmful to a child's sleep.  屏幕时间过长可能有害于孩子的睡眠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 noxious</td><td>/ˈnɑːkʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的，有毒的；产生强烈厌恶的</td><td>noxious fumes<br>有毒的烟气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Noxious fumes from the factory forced the school to close for two days.  工厂排出的有毒烟气迫使学校停课两天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 toxic</td><td>/ˈtɑːksɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有毒的，有害的</td><td>toxic chemicals<br>有毒化学品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The factory dumped toxic waste into the river for years.  这家工厂多年向河里倾倒有毒废料。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>benign</b></td><td>/bɪˈnaɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的</td><td>a benign influence<br>有益无害的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The change in policy proved benign for most small farmers.  事实证明，政策变化对大多数小农户是无害的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harmless</td><td>/ˈhɑːrmləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的，不会造成伤害的</td><td>a harmless little joke<br>无伤大雅的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The snake looks frightening but is completely harmless to humans.  这条蛇看起来可怕，但对人完全无害。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 innocent</td><td>/ˈɪnəsənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的，无恶意的；无辜的</td><td>an innocent remark<br>无恶意的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 What began as an innocent joke ended in a serious quarrel.  一句本无恶意的玩笑最后引发了激烈争吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 innocuous</td><td>/ɪˈnɑːkjuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的；不会引起敌意的</td><td>an innocuous question<br>一个无恶意的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 What seemed an innocuous question turned out to reveal her deepest secret.  看似无恶意的问题最终却揭露了她最深的秘密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inoffensive</td><td>/ˌɪnəˈfensɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的，不讨人厌的</td><td>inoffensive background music<br>不讨人厌的背景音乐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film is bland but inoffensive enough for family viewing.  这部电影平淡，但足够温和，适合全家观看。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 baleful</td><td>/ˈbeɪlfl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的</td><td>a baleful influence<br>有害的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His baleful influence on the young players worried the coach.  他对年轻球员的有害影响让教练担忧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deleterious</td><td>/ˌdeləˈtɪriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的</td><td>deleterious effects on health<br>对健康的有害影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long night shifts had a deleterious effect on the nurses' health.  长期夜班对护士们的健康产生了有害影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 detrimental</td><td>/ˌdetrəˈmentəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的，不利的</td><td>detrimental to your health<br>对你的健康有害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too much screen time is detrimental to a child's eyesight.  过多看屏幕对孩子的视力有害。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 harmful</td><td>/ˈhɑːrmfəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的，造成伤害的</td><td>harmful to health<br>对健康有害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too much screen time can be harmful to a child's sleep.  屏幕时间过长可能有害于孩子的睡眠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 injurious</td><td>/ɪnˈdʒʊriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的，造成伤害的</td><td>injurious to health<br>对健康有害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long hours of night work can be injurious to your health.  长时间夜班工作可能有害健康。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pernicious</td><td>/pərˈnɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的</td><td>a pernicious rumor<br>极有害的谣言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A pernicious rumor about the school spread quickly through the town.  一则关于学校的极有害谣言迅速在镇上传开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>berate</b></td><td>/bɪˈreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉指责，斥责</td><td>berate him publicly<br>公开斥责他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach began to berate his players in front of reporters.  教练开始在记者面前严厉斥责他的球员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 castigate</td><td>/ˈkæstɪɡeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强烈（公开）指责</td><td>castigate for negligence<br>因疏忽而受斥责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge castigated the company for its negligence.  法官严厉指责该公司的疏忽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flay</td><td>/ˈfleɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉批评；剥皮</td><td>flay the officials<br>严厉批评官员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editorial flayed the officials for ignoring repeated safety warnings.  社论严厉批评官员无视再三的安全警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lambaste</td><td>/læmˈbeɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉斥责，痛骂</td><td>lambaste the government<br>痛斥政府</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lambasted the film for its shallow, sentimental ending.  批评家严厉斥责这部电影结局浅薄而煽情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scold</td><td>/ˈskoʊld/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>责骂，训斥</td><td>scold the children<br>责骂孩子们</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher scolded the boys for running through the hallway.  老师因男孩们在走廊跑动而训斥他们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rail</td><td>/reɪl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>痛斥，抱怨</td><td>rail against injustice<br>猛烈抨击不公</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students railed against the unfair new grading policy all week.  学生们整周都在痛斥不公平的新评分政策。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 upbraid</del></td><td>/ʌpˈbreɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（严厉地）谴责，责骂</td><td>upbraid him for lying<br>因撒谎而责骂他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager upbraided him for missing three deadlines in one month.  经理因他一个月内三次误期而严厉责骂他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reproach</td><td>/rɪˈproʊtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>批评，责备</td><td>reproach oneself for<br>因某事自责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She reproached him for never returning her calls.  她责备他从不回她的电话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reprimand</td><td>/ˈreprəˌmænd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>训斥，正式谴责</td><td>reprimand an employee<br>训斥员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The officer was reprimanded for leaving his post early.  这名警官因提早离岗而受到训斥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 commend</td><td>/kəˈmend/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞扬，表扬</td><td>commend her courage<br>赞扬她的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The principal commended her courage in front of the whole school.  校长在全校面前赞扬了她的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 compliment</td><td>/ˈkɑːmplɪmənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞；恭维</td><td>pay someone a compliment<br>赞美某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He paid her a warm compliment on her new painting.  他对她的新画作说了一句诚挚的赞美。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 praise</td><td>/ˈpreɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>赞扬，称赞</td><td>win high praise<br>获得高度赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won high praise from serious critics.  她的第一部小说赢得了严肃评论家的高度赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 3

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>beseech</b></td><td>/bɪˈsiːtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求，哀求</td><td>beseech him for mercy<br>恳求他宽恕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoners beseeched the governor for mercy in a long letter.  囚犯们在一封长信中恳求州长开恩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appeal</td><td>/əˈpiːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>上诉；恳求，呼吁</td><td>file an appeal<br>提出上诉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her lawyers will file an appeal against the verdict next week.  她的律师下周将针对判决提出上诉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conjure</td><td>/kənˈdʒʊr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求，央求；使浮现于脑海</td><td>conjure him to stay<br>恳求他留下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She conjured him to stay until the doctor finally arrived.  她恳求他留下，直到医生终于赶来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 entreat</td><td>/ɪnˈtriːt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求</td><td>entreat her to return<br>恳求她回来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She entreated him to return before their mother learned of the quarrel.  她恳求他在母亲得知这场争吵之前回来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 implore</td><td>/ˌɪmˈplɔːr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恳求，哀求</td><td>implore for help<br>苦苦求助</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She implored the judge to give her brother another chance.  她恳求法官再给她弟弟一次机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 petition</td><td>/pəˈtɪʃn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>请愿书；请求</td><td>sign a petition<br>在请愿书上签名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thousands signed the petition to save the old public library.  数千人签署请愿书，要求保住这座旧的公共图书馆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plead</td><td>/ˈpliːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>恳求；辩护，申辩</td><td>plead for mercy<br>恳求宽恕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoner pleaded for mercy in a trembling, exhausted voice.  囚犯用颤抖疲惫的声音恳求宽恕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supplicate</td><td>/ˈsʌplɪkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>恳求，乞求</td><td>supplicate for forgiveness<br>乞求宽恕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoner knelt to supplicate for forgiveness before the military tribunal.  囚犯跪下，在军事法庭前乞求宽恕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 demand</td><td>/dɪˈmænd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>强烈要求；需求</td><td>demand an apology<br>要求道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The angry customers demanded an apology from the store manager.  愤怒的顾客要求店长道歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>besmirch</b></td><td>/bɪˈsmɜːrtʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诽谤，玷污</td><td>besmirch his reputation<br>诽谤他的名誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sued the paper for trying to besmirch his reputation.  他起诉该报意图诽谤他的名誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 defile</td><td>/dɪˈfaɪl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>玷污，污损；亵渎</td><td>defile a sacred site<br>亵渎圣地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Vandals defiled the ancient temple with paint and broken glass.  破坏者用油漆和碎玻璃亵渎了那座古庙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smear</td><td>/ˈsmɪr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>诽谤，抹黑；涂抹</td><td>a smear campaign<br>抹黑运动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rivals tried to smear the candidate with unfounded financial rumors.  对手试图用毫无根据的财务谣言抹黑这位候选人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 soil</td><td>/ˈsɔɪl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>弄脏，玷污；土壤</td><td>soil his good name<br>玷污他的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One reckless lie soiled a reputation built over many years.  一个轻率的谎言玷污了多年建立的声誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stain</td><td>/ˈsteɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>沾污，玷污；污点</td><td>stain his reputation<br>玷污他的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scandal stained the family name for two whole generations.  这桩丑闻让这个家族的名声蒙污两代人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 smirch</del></td><td>/smɜrtʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>玷污（名誉）</td><td>smirch someone's reputation<br>玷污某人名誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rumor smirched the reputation he had built over thirty years.  谣言玷污了他三十年建立起来的名誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 honor</td><td>/ˈɑːnər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>尊敬，给以荣誉；荣誉</td><td>honor the veterans<br>表彰老兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city honored the veterans with a quiet morning ceremony.  该市用一场安静的晨间仪式表彰老兵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bifurcate</b></td><td>/ˈbaɪfərkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>分叉，分成两支</td><td>bifurcate into two branches<br>分成两条支流</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The path bifurcates into two branches just beyond the wooden bridge.  这条小路在木桥之后分成两条岔路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 divide</td><td>/dɪˈvaɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>分开，划分；分歧</td><td>divide into groups<br>分成小组</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher divided the class into four groups of five.  老师把班级分成四个五人小组。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diverge</td><td>/daɪˈvɜːrdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（使）分叉，散开；分歧</td><td>diverge from the path<br>偏离原有路径</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two trails diverge just beyond the old wooden bridge.  两条小径在那座老木桥之后就分岔开来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fork</td><td>/ˈfɔːrk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>分叉，分岔；叉子</td><td>fork into two roads<br>分成两条路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The trail forks near the old bridge, so read the signs.  小径在旧桥附近分岔，要看清路标。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 coalesce</td><td>/ˌkoʊəˈles/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>合并，融合</td><td>coalesce into a group<br>合并成一个团体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scattered protests coalesced into a national movement.  分散的抗议汇合成一场全国性运动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bigot</b></td><td>/ˈbɪɡət/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>偏执者，固执己见者</td><td>a narrow-minded bigot<br>心胸狭隘的偏执者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a narrow-minded bigot would object to the new neighbors.  只有心胸狭隘的偏执者才会反对新邻居。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dogmatist</del></td><td>/ˈdɔːɡmətɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>固执己见者，教条主义者</td><td>an inflexible dogmatist<br>顽固的教条主义者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor argued like a dogmatist, ignoring every inconvenient fact.  这位教授像教条主义者一样争辩，无视一切不利事实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 partisan</td><td>/ˈpɑːrtəzn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>党派偏见的；盲目拥护者</td><td>partisan politics<br>党派偏见的政治</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The debate quickly sank into partisan shouting about very old grievances.  辩论很快沦为带党派偏见的叫嚷，纠缠陈年旧怨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 depreciator</del></td><td>/dɪˈpriːʃieɪtər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>贬低者，轻视者</td><td>a constant depreciator<br>一贯的贬低者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was a tireless depreciator of other people's real achievements.  他总是不停地贬低他人的真实成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 disparager</del></td><td>/dɪˈspærɪdʒər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>贬低者，毁谤者</td><td>a bitter disparager<br>刻薄的贬低者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics called him a disparager of every new artistic movement.  批评者称他是所有新艺术运动的贬低者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bland</b></td><td>/blænd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无趣的</td><td>a bland performance<br>平淡的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critics called it a bland performance with no real feeling.  评论家称这是一场毫无真情、十分无趣的表演。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 banal</td><td>/bəˈnɑːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非原创的，陈腐的</td><td>banal remarks<br>陈腐的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's ending was so banal that the audience laughed.  这部电影的结尾陈腐得让观众笑了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 sapless</del></td><td>/ˈsæpləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无活力的，枯干的</td><td>a sapless performance<br>毫无生气的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old comedian gave a sapless performance that bored everyone.  这位老喜剧演员的表演毫无生气，让所有人都很无聊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insipid</td><td>/ɪnˈsɪpɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平淡的，无聊的</td><td>an insipid conversation<br>无聊的谈话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film was so insipid that half the audience fell asleep.  这部电影平淡无聊，半数观众都睡着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 riveting</td><td>/ˈrɪvɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的，极迷人的</td><td>a riveting account<br>引人入胜的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary offers a riveting account of life inside the mountain villages.  这部纪录片对山村生活的叙述引人入胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enchanting</td><td>/ɪnˈtʃæntɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，醉人的</td><td>an enchanting melody<br>醉人的旋律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The garden looked enchanting under the soft light of lanterns.  花园在灯笼柔和的光线下显得十分迷人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>blandishment</b></del></td><td>/ˈblændɪʃmənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>甜言蜜语，讨好某人的话</td><td>resist his blandishments<br>抵挡他的甜言蜜语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She resisted every blandishment the salesman could think of.  她抵挡住了推销员能想出的每一句甜言蜜语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flattery</td><td>/ˈflætəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>奉承，谄媚</td><td>shameless flattery<br>无耻的奉承</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He won the manager's favor through steady flattery rather than work.  他靠不断奉承而非工作赢得了经理的青睐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adulation</td><td>/ˌædʒəˈleɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>奉承，谄媚，过度崇拜</td><td>the adulation of fans<br>粉丝的狂热崇拜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young star grew uncomfortable with the constant adulation of crowds.  这位年轻明星对人群不断的追捧感到不适。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>blasé</b></td><td>/blɑːˈzeɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>厌倦的，兴趣索然的</td><td>a blasé attitude<br>满不在乎的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After years of luxury travel he grew blasé about five-star hotels.  多年奢华旅行后，他对五星级酒店已兴趣索然。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indifferent</td><td>/ɪnˈdɪfrənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不感兴趣的，冷漠的；平庸的</td><td>indifferent to criticism<br>对批评漠不关心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is entirely indifferent to what her critics say.  她对批评者说什么完全无所谓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jaded</td><td>/ˈdʒeɪdɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>厌倦的，没兴趣没热情的</td><td>a jaded traveler<br>厌倦旅行的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After twenty countries, even the most enthusiastic traveler becomes jaded and restless.  走过二十个国家后，就算最热情的旅行者也会厌倦而烦躁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unconcerned</td><td>/ˌʌnkənˈsɜːrnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不关心的，漠不关心的</td><td>unconcerned about risk<br>对风险漠不关心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She seemed entirely unconcerned about the noise from the street.  她似乎完全不在意街上的噪音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 world-weary</td><td>/ˈwɜːrld ˈwɪri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>厌世的，厌倦人生的</td><td>a world-weary tone<br>厌世的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His world-weary voice suggested he had seen every possible disappointment.  他厌世的语调显示他已见过各种失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 zealous</td><td>/ˈzeləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情的，充满热忱的</td><td>a zealous supporter<br>热忱的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Zealous volunteers knocked on every door in the small town.  热忱的志愿者敲遍了小镇的每一户人家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fanatic</td><td>/fəˈnætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>狂热者</td><td>a fitness fanatic<br>健身狂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My roommate is a fitness fanatic who trains twice a day.  我室友是个健身狂，一天训练两次。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>blast</b></td><td>/blæst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>爆炸；斥责，抨击</td><td>a bomb blast<br>炸弹爆炸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A bomb blast shattered every window on the narrow street.  一次炸弹爆炸震碎了这条窄街上的每扇窗户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 burst</td><td>/ˈbɜːrst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>爆裂；突发，爆发</td><td>burst into flames<br>突然燃起大火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old pipe burst and flooded the basement within minutes.  旧管子爆裂，几分钟就淹了地下室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 detonation</td><td>/ˌdetəˈneɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>爆炸，引爆</td><td>a loud detonation<br>一声巨大爆炸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sudden detonation shook every window along the narrow street.  突然的爆炸震动了这条窄街上的每扇窗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eruption</td><td>/ɪˈrʌpʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>喷发；爆发</td><td>a volcanic eruption<br>火山喷发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The eruption buried three villages under ash and molten rock.  这次喷发把三个村庄埋在火山灰和熔岩下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outburst</td><td>/ˈaʊtˌbɜːrst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>突然爆发，迸发</td><td>an angry outburst<br>愤怒的爆发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His angry outburst surprised colleagues who thought him unfailingly calm.  他愤怒的爆发让以为他一向冷静的同事很吃惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abuse</td><td>/əˈbjuːz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>辱骂，抨击；不正当或不合理使用</td><td>abuse of power<br>滥用权力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was accused of abusing his position for profit.  他被指控滥用职位谋利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assail</td><td>/əˈseɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抨击，严厉批评</td><td>assail a policy<br>抨击某项政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Opponents assailed the plan as reckless and unfair.  反对者抨击该计划轻率且不公。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 belabor</td><td>/bɪˈleɪbər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>过分强调；痛打</td><td>belabor the point<br>反复纠缠一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is no need to belabor the point any further.  没有必要再对这一点过分强调下去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 castigate</td><td>/ˈkæstɪɡeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强烈（公开）指责</td><td>castigate for negligence<br>因疏忽而受斥责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge castigated the company for its negligence.  法官严厉指责该公司的疏忽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lambaste</td><td>/læmˈbeɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉斥责，痛骂</td><td>lambaste the government<br>痛斥政府</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lambasted the film for its shallow, sentimental ending.  批评家严厉斥责这部电影结局浅薄而煽情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 scathe</del></td><td>/skeɪð/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉批评；伤害</td><td>scathe with bitter sarcasm<br>用讽刺严厉批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reviewers scathed the film for its lazy writing and dull acting.  评论者严厉批评这部电影剧本敷衍、表演乏味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 vituperate</del></td><td>/vaɪˈtuːpəreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>谩骂，责骂</td><td>vituperate an opponent<br>谩骂对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The columnist vituperated the mayor in every weekly article.  这位专栏作家在每周的文章里都痛骂市长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 implosion</td><td>/ˌɪmˈploʊʒən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>向内爆炸；内部崩溃</td><td>the implosion of the market<br>市场的内部崩溃</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The implosion of the housing market ruined thousands of small savers.  楼市的内部崩溃毁掉了成千上万小储户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>blatant</b></td><td>/ˈbleɪtnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，公然的</td><td>a blatant lie<br>赤裸裸的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report exposed a blatant disregard for safety rules.  报告揭露了对安全规则的公然无视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conspicuous</td><td>/kənˈspɪkjuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>显而易见的；吸引人的</td><td>conspicuous consumption<br>炫耀性消费</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His bright red jacket made him conspicuous in the gray winter crowd.  他鲜红的夹克让他在灰暗的冬日人群中格外显眼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flagrant</td><td>/ˈfleɪɡrənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>公然的，明显恶劣的</td><td>a flagrant violation<br>公然的违规</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company committed a flagrant violation of basic safety rules.  这家公司公然违反了基本安全规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glaring</td><td>/ˈɡlerɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，显眼的；耀眼的</td><td>a glaring error<br>明显的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a glaring error on the very first printed page.  印刷的第一页上就有一个明显错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 patent</td><td>/ˈpeɪtnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>显而易见的，明显的；专利</td><td>a patent lie<br>显而易见的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The excuse was a patent lie and everyone knew it.  这个借口是显而易见的谎言，人人都知道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 striking</td><td>/ˈstraɪkɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人注目的，显著的</td><td>a striking resemblance<br>惊人的相似</td></tr>
