<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a striking contrast between the two sisters' styles.  两姐妹的风格形成鲜明的对比。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pronounced</td><td>/prəˈnaʊnst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，显著的</td><td>a pronounced accent<br>明显的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She speaks English with a pronounced accent from the north.  她说英语带着明显的北方口音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 subtle</td><td>/ˈsʌtl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微妙的，难以感知的</td><td>a subtle difference<br>细微的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a subtle difference between these two shades of blue.  这两种蓝色之间有细微的差别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unimpressive</td><td>/ˌʌnɪmˈpresɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平淡无奇的，不起眼的</td><td>an unimpressive debut<br>平淡的首次登场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unimpressive debut gave no hint of the fame ahead.  他平淡的首次登场丝毫看不出日后的名声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>blazon</b></td><td>/ˈbleɪzn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使知名；修饰，装扮</td><td>blazon the news abroad<br>四处宣扬消息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The paper was eager to blazon the news across every front page.  该报急于把这条消息在各家头版上四处宣扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 annunciate</del></td><td>/əˈnʌnsieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>宣布，宣告</td><td>annunciate the verdict<br>宣布判决结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trumpets annunciated the arrival of the queen at the palace gate.  号角宣告女王已抵达宫门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 broadcast</td><td>/ˈbrɔːdˌkæst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>广播，播送；散布</td><td>broadcast the news<br>播报消息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The station broadcast the news of the rescue every hour.  电台每小时播报一次营救消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 declare</td><td>/dɪˈkler/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>宣布，声明，断言</td><td>declare one's intentions<br>表明自己的意图</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge declared the earlier verdict invalid and ordered a retrial.  法官宣布先前判决无效并下令重审。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 publicize</td><td>/ˈpʌblɪsaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>宣传，公布</td><td>publicize the event<br>宣传这场活动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Organizers publicized the concert with posters across the whole campus.  组织者用海报在全校宣传这场音乐会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proclaim</td><td>/prəˈkleɪm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>宣告，使…公之于众</td><td>proclaim independence<br>宣布独立</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The colony voted to proclaim independence from the empire that summer.  那年夏天，殖民地投票宣告脱离帝国独立。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adorn</td><td>/əˈdɔːrn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>装饰，点缀</td><td>adorn the walls<br>装饰墙面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old maps and photographs adorn the walls of the study.  旧地图和照片装饰着书房的墙壁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 bedeck</del></td><td>/bɪˈdek/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>装饰，点缀</td><td>bedeck the hall with flowers<br>用花装饰大厅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers bedeck the hall with flowers before every summer festival.  每年夏季节庆前，村民都用花装饰大厅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 embellish</td><td>/ɪmˈbelɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>装饰，修饰；添枝加叶</td><td>embellish a story<br>给故事添枝加叶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He embellished the tale with details that never actually happened.  他给故事添加了从未发生过的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 garnish</td><td>/ˈɡɑːrnɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>装饰，点缀（食物）</td><td>garnish with fresh herbs<br>用新鲜香草点缀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chef garnished each plate with parsley and thin lemon slices.  厨师用欧芹和薄柠檬片点缀每个盘子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 withhold</td><td>/wɪðˈhoʊld/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抑制；扣压，不给予</td><td>withhold information<br>隐瞒信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was accused of withholding key information.  他被指控隐瞒关键信息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 blemish</td><td>/ˈblemɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>缺点，污点；损害，玷污</td><td>a blemish on his record<br>他履历上的污点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One late payment left a small blemish on his credit record.  一次逾期付款在他的信用记录上留下了小小的污点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deface</td><td>/dɪˈfeɪs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>损毁外观，涂污</td><td>deface a monument<br>损毁纪念碑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone defaced the statue with spray paint during the night.  有人夜里用喷漆涂污了那座雕像。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mar</td><td>/mɑːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>破坏，削弱</td><td>mar the celebration<br>破坏庆典气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A careless remark at dinner managed to mar an otherwise perfect evening.  餐桌上一句轻率的话毁掉了本该完美的一晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 spoil</td><td>/ˈspɔɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>损坏，破坏；溺爱</td><td>spoil the surprise<br>破坏惊喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy rain spoiled the picnic we had planned for weeks.  大雨破坏了我们筹划数周的野餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 4

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>blemish</b></td><td>/ˈblemɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>缺点，污点；损害，玷污</td><td>a blemish on his record<br>他履历上的污点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One late payment left a small blemish on his credit record.  一次逾期付款在他的信用记录上留下了小小的污点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blotch</td><td>/ˈblɑːtʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>斑点，污渍；使起斑</td><td>red blotches on skin<br>皮肤上的红斑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ink blotches covered the corner of the carefully written letter.  墨渍污了那封精心书写信件的一角。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 defect</td><td>/ˈdiːfekt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>缺陷，瑕疵；叛逃</td><td>a manufacturing defect<br>制造缺陷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A tiny defect in the wiring caused the entire system failure.  线路上一个微小缺陷导致了整个系统故障。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fault</td><td>/ˈfɔːlt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>缺点，过错；断层</td><td>find fault with<br>挑剔，找毛病</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His only fault is that he never admits being wrong.  他唯一的缺点是从不承认自己错了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flaw</td><td>/flɔː/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>瑕疵，缺点；降低，破坏，使不完美</td><td>a fatal flaw<br>致命的缺陷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One small flaw in the design caused the bridge to fail.  设计中一个小缺陷导致桥梁失效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mar</td><td>/mɑːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>破坏，削弱</td><td>mar the celebration<br>破坏庆典气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A careless remark at dinner managed to mar an otherwise perfect evening.  餐桌上一句轻率的话毁掉了本该完美的一晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spot</td><td>/ˈspɑːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>污点，斑点；地点</td><td>a spot on the shirt<br>衬衫上的污点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A grease spot on the sleeve ruined his only white shirt.  袖子上的油污弄坏了他唯一的白衬衫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scar</td><td>/ˈskɑːr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>伤疤，创痕；留下伤痕</td><td>leave a deep scar<br>留下深深伤疤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The accident left a thin scar above his left eyebrow.  那次事故在他左眉上方留下一道细疤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 break</td><td>/ˈbreɪk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>损坏，打破；间歇</td><td>break the vase<br>打破花瓶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cat broke the vase while chasing a moth indoors.  猫在屋里追蛾子时打破了花瓶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disfigure</td><td>/dɪsˈfɪɡjər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>损毁外形，使难看</td><td>disfigure the facade<br>损毁建筑正面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap repairs disfigured the elegant facade of the old theater.  廉价的修补损毁了老剧院优雅的正面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harm</td><td>/ˈhɑːrm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>伤害，损害</td><td>harm the environment<br>损害环境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Pesticide runoff harms the fish living in the nearby stream.  农药径流危害了附近溪流中的鱼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hurt</td><td>/ˈhɜːrt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>伤害，使疼痛</td><td>hurt his feelings<br>伤害他的感情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Your careless comment clearly hurt her feelings at the dinner.  你随口的评论显然在晚餐上伤了她的感情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impair</td><td>/ɪmˈper/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>损害，削弱</td><td>impair his judgment<br>削弱他的判断力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lack of sleep seriously impairs judgment and slows your reactions.  睡眠不足严重损害判断力并减慢反应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 injure</td><td>/ˈɪndʒər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使受伤，伤害</td><td>injure his knee<br>伤到他的膝盖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He injured his knee while running downhill on wet stones.  他在湿滑石头上跑下坡时伤了膝盖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spoil</td><td>/ˈspɔɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>损坏，破坏；溺爱</td><td>spoil the surprise<br>破坏惊喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy rain spoiled the picnic we had planned for weeks.  大雨破坏了我们筹划数周的野餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vitiate</td><td>/ˈvɪʃieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>削弱，损害；使无效</td><td>vitiate an agreement<br>使协议失效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single false statement can vitiate the entire contract.  一句虚假陈述就能使整份合同失效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fix</td><td>/ˈfɪks/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>修理，解决；固定</td><td>fix the leaking pipe<br>修好漏水的管子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A neighbor fixed the leaking pipe before the floor was ruined.  邻居在地板毁坏前修好了漏水的管子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 renovate</td><td>/ˈrenəveɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>修复，维修</td><td>renovate the old house<br>翻修老房子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We must renovate the old house completely before moving in.  搬进去之前我们必须彻底翻修这栋老房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repair</td><td>/rɪˈper/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>修理，修补</td><td>repair the roof<br>修补屋顶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Workers repaired the roof just before the rainy season began.  工人们在雨季开始前修好了屋顶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 revamp</td><td>/riˈvæmp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>改进，翻新，改造</td><td>revamp the website<br>改版网站</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company revamped its website to attract younger overseas customers.  公司改版网站以吸引更年轻的海外顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>blight</b></td><td>/blaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）枯萎；损害</td><td>blight the harvest<br>使收成枯萎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy rain and disease can blight the harvest in a single week.  暴雨和病害能在一周之内毁掉收成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wither</td><td>/ˈwɪðər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>枯萎，凋谢</td><td>wither in the heat<br>在高温中枯萎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tomato plants withered after a week without rain or shade.  一周无雨又没有遮荫，番茄苗都枯萎了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shrivel</td><td>/ˈʃrɪvəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>皱缩，枯萎，干瘪</td><td>shrivel in the sun<br>在阳光下皱缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unwatered seedlings shriveled within two days of intense heat.  没浇水的幼苗在两天酷热中就干瘪了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 damage</td><td>/ˈdæmədʒ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>损害，破坏；损失</td><td>damage the crops<br>损害庄稼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Late frost damaged the crops across the entire river valley.  晚霜损害了整个河谷的庄稼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deteriorate</td><td>/dɪˈtɪriəreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>恶化，变坏</td><td>deteriorate rapidly<br>迅速恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her health deteriorated sharply during the cold winter.  在寒冷的冬天里她的健康急剧恶化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harm</td><td>/ˈhɑːrm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>伤害，损害</td><td>harm the environment<br>损害环境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Pesticide runoff harms the fish living in the nearby stream.  农药径流危害了附近溪流中的鱼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impair</td><td>/ɪmˈper/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>损害，削弱</td><td>impair his judgment<br>削弱他的判断力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lack of sleep seriously impairs judgment and slows your reactions.  睡眠不足严重损害判断力并减慢反应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mar</td><td>/mɑːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>破坏，削弱</td><td>mar the celebration<br>破坏庆典气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A careless remark at dinner managed to mar an otherwise perfect evening.  餐桌上一句轻率的话毁掉了本该完美的一晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ruin</td><td>/ˈruːɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>毁坏，毁灭；废墟</td><td>ruin the harvest<br>毁掉收成</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Weeks of drought ruined the harvest and emptied the village granary.  数周干旱毁掉收成，村里粮仓也空了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 flourish</td><td>/ˈflɜːrɪʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>茂盛；繁荣</td><td>flourish in warm climates<br>在温暖气候中繁茂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Olive trees flourish in the dry, sunny hills of southern Spain.  橄榄树在西班牙南部干燥向阳的山丘上长得茂盛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bliss</b></td><td>/blɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>极度快乐</td><td>domestic bliss<br>家庭幸福</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They spent a year of quiet domestic bliss in the countryside.  他们在乡下度过了一年宁静而极度幸福的家庭生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beatitude</td><td>/biˈætɪtuːd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>至福，极乐</td><td>a state of beatitude<br>极乐的状态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old monk's face wore an expression of quiet beatitude.  那位老僧脸上带着宁静至福的神情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 joy</td><td>/ˈdʒɔɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>欢乐，喜悦</td><td>jump for joy<br>高兴得跳起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The news of his recovery filled the whole family with joy.  他康复的消息让全家人充满喜悦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ecstasy</td><td>/ˈekstəsi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>狂喜</td><td>in a state of ecstasy<br>处于狂喜之中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd rose in a state of ecstasy when the winning goal went in.  制胜球进网时，全场观众狂喜地站了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elation</td><td>/ɪˈleɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>得意，兴高采烈</td><td>a feeling of elation<br>兴高采烈之感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her elation faded once she read the fine print carefully.  她仔细读了小字条款后，兴奋就消退了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 grief</td><td>/ˈɡriːf/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>悲痛，哀伤</td><td>overcome with grief<br>悲痛不已</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was silent with grief for weeks after her father died.  父亲去世后，她数周沉默悲痛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 misery</td><td>/ˈmɪzəri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>痛苦，苦难</td><td>live in misery<br>生活在痛苦中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The war brought years of misery to ordinary families.  战争给普通家庭带来了多年的苦难。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>blithe</b></td><td>/blaɪð/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愉快高兴的；无忧无虑的；漫不经心的</td><td>a blithe disregard<br>漫不经心的无视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His blithe disregard for the rules annoyed the older staff.  他对规则漫不经心的无视让资深员工不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bright</td><td>/ˈbraɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>快活的，开朗的；明亮的</td><td>a bright smile<br>开朗的笑容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She greeted every customer with a bright and genuine smile.  她用开朗真诚的笑容迎接每位顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 buoyant</td><td>/ˈbuːjənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>心情好的</td><td>in a buoyant mood<br>心情愉快</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived in a buoyant mood after hearing the good news.  听到好消息后，他心情愉快地到了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gay</td><td>/ˈɡeɪ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>快乐的，欢快的</td><td>gay laughter<br>欢快的笑声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Gay music and bright lanterns filled the village square that evening.  那晚欢快的音乐和明亮的灯笼充满了村中广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jocular</td><td>/ˈdʒɑːkjələr/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>搞笑的，欢乐的</td><td>a jocular remark<br>诙谐的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His jocular tone kept the tense meeting from turning truly hostile.  他诙谐的语气让紧张的会议没有真正翻脸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 jocund</del></td><td>/ˈdʒɑːkənd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢快的，高兴的</td><td>a jocund gathering<br>欢快的聚会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The jocund crowd sang and danced until the last lantern went out.  欢快的人群唱歌跳舞，直到最后一盏灯笼熄灭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jovial</td><td>/ˈdʒoʊviəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愉快的</td><td>a jovial host<br>愉快的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our jovial host kept the guests laughing throughout the long winter evening.  我们愉快的主人让客人们在漫长的冬夜里笑声不断。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debonair</td><td>/ˌdebəˈner/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>风度翩翩的，温文尔雅的</td><td>a debonair manner<br>从容优雅的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The debonair gentleman charmed every woman in the crowded ballroom.  这位风度翩翩的绅士让拥挤舞厅里的每位女士都为之倾倒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insouciant</td><td>/ɪnˈsuːsiənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无忧虑的，不在乎的</td><td>an insouciant shrug<br>满不在乎地耸肩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered the harsh criticism with an insouciant shrug and a smile.  他对严厉的批评只是满不在乎地耸耸肩，笑了笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lighthearted</td><td>/ˌlaɪtˈhɑːrtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻松愉快的，无忧无虑的</td><td>a lighthearted comedy<br>轻松的喜剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We wanted a lighthearted film after such a stressful week.  在如此紧张的一周后，我们想看部轻松的电影。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dour</td><td>/ˈdaʊər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严厉的</td><td>a dour expression<br>严厉的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guard's dour expression discouraged anyone from asking him for directions.  警卫严厉的表情让人不敢向他问路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gloomy</td><td>/ˈɡluːmi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧郁的</td><td>a gloomy mood<br>忧郁的心情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She grew gloomy whenever the winter rain lasted more than a week.  每当冬雨连下一周以上，她就变得忧郁起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 morose</td><td>/məˈroʊs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧郁的</td><td>grow morose and silent<br>变得忧郁沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After losing the election he grew morose and rarely left his apartment.  落选之后他变得忧郁，很少离开公寓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 saturnine</td><td>/ˈsætərnaɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧郁的，阴沉的</td><td>a saturnine temperament<br>阴沉的性情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His saturnine expression discouraged anyone from starting a cheerful conversation.  他阴沉的表情让人不敢开启轻松的话题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sulky</td><td>/ˈsʌlki/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>赌气的，生气不说话的</td><td>a sulky teenager<br>赌气的少年</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sulky child refused to speak for the whole long afternoon.  那个赌气的孩子整个下午都不肯说话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sullen</td><td>/ˈsʌlən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>闷闷不乐的</td><td>a sullen silence<br>闷闷不乐的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sullen silence fell over the table when the topic was raised.  提到这个话题时，餐桌上陷入闷闷不乐的沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 careworn</td><td>/ˈkerwɔːrn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>憔悴的，饱受忧虑的</td><td>a careworn face<br>憔悴的面容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her careworn face brightened when the children ran into the room.  孩子们跑进房间时，她憔悴的脸上亮了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>blueprint</b></td><td>/ˈbluːprɪnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>蓝图，行动计划；事先规划</td><td>a blueprint for reform<br>改革的蓝图</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee published a blueprint for reform of the tax system.  委员会公布了税制改革的蓝图。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arrange</td><td>/əˈreɪndʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>安排，筹划；整理</td><td>arrange the meeting<br>安排会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She arranged the whole trip in a single busy afternoon.  她在一个忙碌的下午就安排好了整趟行程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 budget</td><td>/ˈbʌdʒɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>编制预算，谋划；预算</td><td>budget for the trip<br>为旅行做预算</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We budgeted carefully for the trip and still ran short.  我们为旅行仔细做了预算，钱还是不够。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 calculate</td><td>/ˈkælkjəˌleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>计算；筹划，谋划</td><td>calculate the cost<br>计算成本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers calculated the cost of the bridge down to the last bolt.  工程师把这座桥的成本精确算到了最后一颗螺栓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 organize</td><td>/ˈɔːrɡəˌnaɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>组织，筹划，安排</td><td>organize the conference<br>筹办会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Three students organized the conference without any help from staff.  三名学生在没有职工帮助下筹办了这次会议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frame</td><td>/ˈfreɪm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>制定，构想；框架</td><td>frame a new policy<br>制定新政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee framed a new policy on remote work overnight.  委员会一夜之间制定了新的远程工作政策。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lay out</td><td>/leɪ ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>规划，布置；详细说明</td><td>lay out a plan<br>拟定一份计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She laid out the whole plan in fewer than ten minutes.  她用不到十分钟就把整个计划说清楚了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>blunder</b></td><td>/ˈblʌndər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>大错，失误；犯大错</td><td>a costly blunder<br>代价高昂的失误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sending the letter unsigned was a costly blunder.  把信未签名就寄出是个代价高昂的失误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mistake</td><td>/mɪˈsteɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>错误，过失；误解</td><td>make a mistake<br>犯错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everyone makes mistakes, but few admit them so quickly.  人人都会犯错，但很少有人这么快承认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gaffe</td><td>/ɡæf/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（社交上）失礼，失态</td><td>a diplomatic gaffe<br>外交失礼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His remark about the queen was a serious diplomatic gaffe.  他关于女王的言论是严重的外交失礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lapse</td><td>/læps/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>小过失</td><td>a lapse of memory<br>记忆上的小失误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A brief lapse of memory made him forget his colleague's name entirely.  一时的记忆失误使他完全忘了同事的名字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 error</td><td>/ˈerər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>错误，差错</td><td>a clerical error<br>笔误，书写错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single error in the formula ruined the entire calculation.  公式中一个错误就毁了整个计算。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>blunt</b></td><td>/blʌnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>直言不讳的；钝的</td><td>a blunt refusal<br>直率的拒绝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her blunt refusal left no room for further discussion.  她直率的拒绝没给进一步讨论留下余地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dull</td><td>/dʌl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迟钝的；乏味的</td><td>a dull student<br>迟钝的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture was so dull that half the class slept.  讲座乏味得让一半学生睡着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deaden</td><td>/ˈdedən/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使减弱，使麻木</td><td>deaden the pain<br>减轻疼痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick carpets deaden the sound of footsteps upstairs.  厚地毯减弱了楼上的脚步声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 hebetate</del></td><td>/ˈhebɪteɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使迟钝，变迟钝</td><td>hebetate the senses<br>使感官变迟钝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of routine labor seemed to hebetate his once quick mind.  多年例行劳作似乎让他曾经敏捷的头脑变迟钝了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 benumb</del></td><td>/bɪˈnʌm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使麻木，使迟钝</td><td>benumb the fingers<br>使手指麻木</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The freezing wind benumbed our fingers within a few short minutes.  刺骨寒风几分钟就让我们的手指麻木了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 enfeeble</del></td><td>/ɪnˈfiːbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使衰弱，使虚弱</td><td>enfeeble the patient<br>使病人虚弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A long fever enfeebled him so much that walking hurt.  长期发烧使他虚弱到走路都疼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abrupt</td><td>/əˈbrʌpt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>突然的；言语生硬的</td><td>an abrupt reply<br>生硬的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His abrupt answer ended the conversation before it really began.  他生硬的回答让谈话还没开始就结束了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bluff</td><td>/blʌf/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>虚张声势地骗人，唬人</td><td>bluff your way through<br>靠虚张声势混过去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to bluff his way through the whole interview.  他试图靠虚张声势混过整场面试。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brusque</td><td>/brʌsk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>唐突无礼的，简慢的</td><td>a brusque manner<br>唐突的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clerk's brusque manner offended nearly every waiting customer.  那名职员唐突的态度几乎冒犯了所有等候的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curt</td><td>/kɜːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>言词简略的，直接（以至显得粗鲁）的</td><td>a curt answer<br>简慢的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her curt reply made it clear that the discussion was over.  她简慢的回答清楚表明讨论已经结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gruff</td><td>/ɡrʌf/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>生硬粗暴的；声音低沉的</td><td>a gruff voice<br>低沉粗哑的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A gruff old sailor pointed silently toward the harbor entrance.  一位声音低沉的老水手默默指向港口入口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 whet</td><td>/wet/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刺激，激起</td><td>whet the appetite<br>激起食欲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The smell of garlic whetted our appetite long before dinner.  蒜香早在开饭前就激起了我们的食欲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sharpen</td><td>/ˈʃɑːrpən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使锋利；使敏锐</td><td>sharpen the knife<br>磨快刀子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sharpened the knife on a wet stone before cutting bread.  他在湿石上磨快刀子后才切面包。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 circuitous</td><td>/sərˈkjuːɪtəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迂回的，不直接的</td><td>a circuitous route<br>迂回的路线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We took a circuitous route to avoid the flooded highway.  我们绕了一条迂回的路以避开被淹的公路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mealy-mouthed</td><td>/ˈmiːli ˈmaʊðd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>说话拐弯抹角的</td><td>a mealy-mouthed apology<br>含糊其词的道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters distrust mealy-mouthed politicians who never give a straight answer.  选民不信任那些从不直接回答、说话含糊的政客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>blur</b></td><td>/blɜːr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>（使）变得朦胧，（使）变得不清楚；使不易理解</td><td>blur the distinction<br>模糊了界限</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New technology tends to blur the distinction between work and home.  新技术往往模糊了工作与家庭的界限。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shroud</td><td>/ʃraʊd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>将…从视线中隔离，遮蔽</td><td>shrouded in mist<br>笼罩在雾中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick mist shrouded the harbor and hid the ships from view.  浓雾笼罩港口，把船只从视线中遮住。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 becloud</del></td><td>/bɪˈklaʊd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使模糊，使混乱</td><td>becloud the issue<br>混淆问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Emotional appeals often becloud the real issue under serious discussion.  情感诉求常常把正在认真讨论的真问题弄模糊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 befog</del></td><td>/bɪˈfɔːɡ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使模糊，使困惑</td><td>befog the argument<br>使论证变模糊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Technical jargon befogs the argument instead of making it clearer.  技术术语让论证更模糊，而非更清楚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obscure</td><td>/əbˈskjʊr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>含义模糊的；平凡的，不知名的</td><td>an obscure poet<br>不知名的诗人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained an obscure poet until a critic rediscovered his work decades later.  他一直是个不知名的诗人，直到几十年后一位批评家重新发现他的作品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obfuscate</td><td>/ˈɑːbfʌskeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑，使模糊</td><td>obfuscate the facts<br>把事实弄模糊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness seemed determined to obfuscate the facts rather than explain them.  证人似乎一心要把事实弄模糊，而不是解释清楚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clear</td><td>/ˈklɪr/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，确凿的；清楚的</td><td>a clear majority<br>明显的多数</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party won by a clear majority of votes.  该党以明显多数票获胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clarify</td><td>/ˈklerɪfaɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阐明，澄清</td><td>clarify the rules<br>澄清规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dean clarified the rules after weeks of confused complaints.  在数周混乱的投诉后，院长澄清了规则。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 illuminate</td><td>/ɪˈluːmɪneɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阐明</td><td>illuminate the problem<br>阐明问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her lecture illuminated a problem that had puzzled economists for years.  她的讲座阐明了一个困扰经济学家多年的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>blurt</b></td><td>/blɜːrt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>突然说出，冲动地说</td><td>blurt out the secret<br>突然说出秘密</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He almost blurted out the secret in front of her parents.  他差点在她父母面前突然说出秘密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 burst</td><td>/ˈbɜːrst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>爆裂；突发，爆发</td><td>burst into flames<br>突然燃起大火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old pipe burst and flooded the basement within minutes.  旧管子爆裂，几分钟就淹了地下室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bolt</td><td>/ˈboʊlt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>突然说出；猛冲</td><td>bolt out an answer<br>脱口说出答案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He bolted out the secret before anyone could stop him.  他还没等人阻止就把秘密说了出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ejaculate</td><td>/ɪˈdʒækjəleɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>突然喊出，脱口而出</td><td>ejaculate a warning<br>突然喊出警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She ejaculated a warning just before the heavy branch struck the roof.  就在粗树枝砸到屋顶前，她突然喊出一声警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cry out</td><td>/kraɪ ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>大声呼喊，惊叫</td><td>cry out in pain<br>痛得大叫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had to cry out twice before the guard finally heard her.  她不得不大声呼喊两次，警卫才终于听见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 muffle</td><td>/ˈmʌfl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>压低声音，包裹使消声</td><td>muffle the sound<br>把声音压低</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick curtains muffle the sound of traffic outside the window.  厚窗帘减弱了窗外的车流声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mute</td><td>/mjuːt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不说话的，缄默的</td><td>remain mute on the issue<br>对此事保持缄默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained mute on the issue despite repeated questions from reporters.  尽管记者反复提问，他对此事仍保持沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bluster</b></td><td>/ˈblʌstər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>狂妄自大地大声说；喧闹的状态</td><td>bluster and threaten<br>大声吓唬威胁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager began to bluster and threaten when questioned closely.  被仔细追问时，经理开始狂妄自大地大声吓唬威胁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 roar</td><td>/ˈrɔːr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>咆哮，怒吼；轰鸣</td><td>roar with anger<br>愤怒地咆哮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach roared at the players from the sideline.  教练在场边对球员们大声咆哮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clamor</td><td>/ˈklæmər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>喧闹，喧哗，噪声</td><td>a clamor of voices<br>一片人声喧哗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A clamor of voices rose from the crowded courtyard.  拥挤的院子里传出一片人声喧哗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rattle</td><td>/ˈrætəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>发出咔嗒声；使慌乱</td><td>rattle the windows<br>把窗户震得作响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The passing train rattled every window in the old house.  驶过的火车震得老屋的每扇窗都咔咔作响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disturbance</td><td>/dɪˈstɜːrbəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>骚乱，扰乱</td><td>cause a disturbance<br>引起骚乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police were called after a disturbance broke out in the bar.  酒吧里发生骚乱后警察被叫来了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pandemonium</td><td>/ˌpændɪˈmoʊniəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>喧嚣，一片混乱</td><td>pandemonium broke out<br>顿时一片混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Pandemonium broke out when the final goal was scored.  最后一个进球时全场一片沸腾混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tumult</td><td>/ˈtuːmʌlt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骚动，暴动</td><td>the tumult of the crowd<br>人群的骚动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tumult in the square grew louder as more protesters arrived.  随着更多抗议者到来，广场上的骚动越来越大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 turmoil</td><td>/ˈtɜːrmɔɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，动荡，骚动</td><td>in political turmoil<br>处于政治动荡中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The country remained in political turmoil for nearly a decade.  这个国家在近十年里一直处于政治动荡之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 whisper</td><td>/ˈwɪspər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>低声说，耳语</td><td>whisper in her ear<br>在她耳边低语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She whispered the answer so the teacher would not hear.  她低声说出答案，以免老师听见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 5

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>boggle</b></td><td>/ˈbɑːɡl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>（因为怀疑、恐惧）犹豫</td><td>boggle at the risk<br>因风险而犹豫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investors still boggle at the risk of such a large loan.  投资者仍因如此大额贷款的风险而犹豫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hesitate</td><td>/ˈhezəˌteɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>犹豫，迟疑</td><td>hesitate before answering<br>回答前犹豫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not hesitate to call me if the plan changes.  如果计划有变，别犹豫，给我打电话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 falter</td><td>/ˈfɔːltər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>蹒跚；犹豫，踌躇</td><td>falter in his resolve<br>决心动摇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Mr. Garrison never faltered in his resolve to finish the project.  加里森先生完成这个项目的决心从未动摇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 waver</td><td>/ˈweɪvər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>动摇，犹豫不决</td><td>waver between two options<br>在两个选项间摇摆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wavered for weeks before accepting the job offer.  他犹豫了好几周才接受这份工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>boisterous</b></td><td>/ˈbɔɪstərəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>喧嚷的，吵闹的</td><td>a boisterous crowd<br>喧闹的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A boisterous crowd filled the square outside the stadium.  一群喧闹的人挤满了体育场外的广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rowdy</td><td>/ˈraʊdi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吵闹的，撒野的</td><td>a rowdy party<br>闹翻天的派对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rowdy fans kept the whole neighborhood awake.  吵闹的球迷让整个街区都睡不着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vociferous</td><td>/voʊˈsɪfərəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>喧哗的，大叫大嚷的</td><td>a vociferous critic<br>大声抗议的批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She became a vociferous opponent of the new tax law.  她成了新税法的大声反对者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blatant</td><td>/ˈbleɪtnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，公然的</td><td>a blatant lie<br>赤裸裸的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report exposed a blatant disregard for safety rules.  报告揭露了对安全规则的公然无视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clamorous</td><td>/ˈklæmərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>喧闹的；大声要求的</td><td>clamorous demands for reform<br>高声要求改革</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clamorous market made quiet conversation impossible.  喧闹的市场让人根本无法安静交谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 raucous</td><td>/ˈrɔːkəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>刺耳的，喧闹的</td><td>raucous laughter<br>刺耳的大笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Raucous laughter spilled out of the crowded pub.  刺耳的笑声从挤满人的酒吧里传出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rambunctious</td><td>/ræmˈbʌŋkʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>喧闹失控的，撒欢的</td><td>rambunctious children<br>闹得没边的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Three rambunctious puppies knocked over the coffee table.  三只闹腾的小狗把茶几撞翻了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quiet</td><td>/ˈkwaɪət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>安静的；平静的</td><td>keep the room quiet<br>保持房间安静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library stays quiet even during exam week.  即使在考试周，图书馆也保持安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sedate</td><td>/sɪˈdeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>淡定的，安静的</td><td>a sedate atmosphere<br>安静沉稳的气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hotel keeps a sedate atmosphere that older guests clearly appreciate.  这家酒店保持着安静沉稳的气氛，年长客人显然很喜欢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bolster</b></td><td>/ˈboʊlstər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支持，增强</td><td>bolster his confidence<br>增强他的信心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A word of praise from her would bolster his confidence enormously.  她一句赞扬就会极大地增强他的信心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brace</td><td>/ˈbreɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使振作，鼓励；支撑</td><td>brace yourself for impact<br>做好承受冲击准备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A cup of hot tea braced him for the long night.  一杯热茶让他为漫长的夜晚振作起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 buttress</td><td>/ˈbʌtrɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支持，加强（论点）</td><td>buttress an argument<br>为论点提供支撑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fresh evidence buttressed the prosecutor's case against the two suspects.  新证据支持了检察官针对两名嫌疑人的指控。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bear</td><td>/ˈber/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>承受，支撑；忍受</td><td>bear the weight<br>承受重量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These thin beams cannot bear the weight of the roof.  这些细梁承受不了屋顶的重量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sustain</td><td>/səˈsteɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支撑；维持</td><td>sustain economic growth<br>维持经济增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The country cannot sustain such growth without more energy.  没有更多能源，这个国家无法维持这样的增长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 undergird</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈɡɜːrd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支撑，为…打下基础</td><td>undergird the theory<br>为理论打牢基础</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Careful field data undergird every claim made in her new book.  细致的实地数据为她新书中的每一个论断打下了基础。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 underpin</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈpɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支撑，构成…的基础</td><td>underpin the whole theory<br>支撑整个理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Careful field data underpin every claim in this paper.  细致的实地数据支撑着本文的每一个论断。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uphold</td><td>/ʌpˈhoʊld/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支持，赞成；维持（原判）</td><td>uphold a decision<br>维持原判决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The appeals court chose to uphold the original ruling in full.  上诉法院决定完全维持原判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prop up</td><td>/prɑːp ˈʌp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>支撑，扶持</td><td>prop up the failing bank<br>扶持濒危银行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The government spent billions to prop up the failing bank.  政府花了数十亿来扶持这家濒临倒闭的银行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 buoy</td><td>/ˈbuːi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使充满勇气和力量，使振作</td><td>buoy his spirits<br>使他振作起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Kind letters from strangers buoyed her spirits during the long illness.  陌生人的暖心来信在她久病期间让她振作起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reinforce</td><td>/ˌriːɪnˈfɔːrs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加强，强化</td><td>reinforce the message<br>强化这一信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Repeated praise reinforced the child's interest in reading.  反复的表扬强化了孩子对阅读的兴趣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dampen</td><td>/ˈdæmpən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抑制，压抑（感情、精力等），泼冷水</td><td>dampen his enthusiasm<br>打击他的热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Losing the first game did nothing to dampen the team's spirits.  首场失利丝毫没有打击球队的士气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bombast</b></td><td>/ˈbɑːmbæst/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>夸大的言辞</td><td>full of bombast<br>满口夸大之词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech was full of bombast but offered no concrete plan.  那篇演讲充满浮夸之词，却没有具体的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 braggadocio</td><td>/ˌbræɡəˈdoʊʃioʊ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>自夸，吹牛</td><td>empty braggadocio<br>空洞的吹牛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech was pure braggadocio with no real plan behind it.  他的演讲纯是吹牛，背后没有真正的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 grandiloquence</del></td><td>/ɡrænˈdɪləkwəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>浮夸的言辞</td><td>empty grandiloquence<br>空洞的浮夸辞藻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The senator's grandiloquence impressed few of the tired voters.  这位参议员的浮夸辞令没打动多少疲惫的选民。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exaggeration</td><td>/ɪɡˌzædʒəˈreɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>夸张，夸大</td><td>a slight exaggeration<br>略有夸张</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 To call the film a masterpiece is an exaggeration.  把这部电影称为杰作是一种夸张。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 understatement</td><td>/ˈʌndərˌsteɪtmənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>保守的说法，轻描淡写</td><td>a masterpiece of understatement<br>轻描淡写的典范</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Saying the room was messy is a serious understatement.  说这房间乱，实在是太保守了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bonhomie</b></td><td>/ˌbɑːnəˈmiː/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>友好和气，亲切热情</td><td>an air of bonhomie<br>和蔼亲切的气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The host greeted every guest with easy bonhomie.  主人以随和亲切的态度招待每一位客人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affability</td><td>/ˌæfəˈbɪləti/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>和蔼可亲，友善</td><td>unfailing affability<br>始终如一的和蔼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His affability made even nervous newcomers feel at ease.  他的和蔼可亲让紧张的新人也感到自在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 amiability</td><td>/ˌeɪmiəˈbɪləti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>和蔼可亲，友善</td><td>her natural amiability<br>她天生的和善</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her amiability won over even the most difficult clients.  她的和善赢得了连最难缠的客户的好感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 geniality</td><td>/ˌdʒiːniˈæləti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>亲切，友好和气</td><td>unfailing geniality<br>始终不变的亲切</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old professor treated students with unfailing geniality.  这位老教授对学生始终亲切和气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>boo</b></td><td>/ˈbuː/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>嘘（以表示不满或嘲笑）</td><td>boo the referee<br>对裁判喝倒彩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fans began to boo the referee after the second penalty.  第二次判罚之后，球迷开始对裁判发出嘘声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jeer</td><td>/ˈdʒɪr/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>嘲笑，讥讽</td><td>jeer at the loser<br>嘲笑失败者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd jeered at the player who missed the penalty.  人群嘲笑那名罚失点球的球员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scorn</td><td>/skɔːrn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>轻蔑，鄙视，不屑</td><td>scorn an offer<br>不屑接受提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He scorned the idea of asking his parents for money.  他不屑于向父母要钱这个想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 applaud</td><td>/əˈplɔːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>称赞，赞成；鼓掌</td><td>applaud the decision<br>赞成这一决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teachers applauded the decision to reduce homework for young pupils.  老师们赞成给低年级学生减少作业的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>boon</b></td><td>/buːn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恩惠，福利</td><td>a boon to farmers<br>对农民的福音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This solar charger is a real boon to travelling photographers.  这款太阳能充电器对旅行摄影师来说是一大福音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gift</td><td>/ˈɡɪft/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>礼物；天赋</td><td>a generous gift<br>一份丰厚的礼物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library received a generous gift from a former student.  图书馆收到一位老校友的丰厚捐赠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 benevolence</td><td>/bəˈnevələns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>善心，仁慈；善举</td><td>an act of benevolence<br>一次善举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hospital survives on the benevolence of local donors.  这家医院靠当地捐助者的善心维持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 present</td><td>/ˈprezənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>礼物；赠送</td><td>a birthday present<br>一份生日礼物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wrapped the birthday present in bright blue paper.  她用亮蓝色的纸包好了生日礼物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 windfall</td><td>/ˈwɪndfɔːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>意外之财；意外的好运</td><td>an unexpected windfall<br>意外之财</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unexpected windfall allowed them to repay the whole loan early.  这笔意外之财让他们提前还清了全部贷款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 misfortune</td><td>/mɪsˈfɔːrtʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>不幸，灾祸</td><td>suffer a misfortune<br>遭遇不幸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A series of misfortunes left the family deeply in debt.  一连串的不幸让这家人负债累累。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scourge</td><td>/skɜːrdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>祸患，灾难；鞭子</td><td>the scourge of famine<br>饥荒之祸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Famine was the scourge of the region for a whole decade.  饥荒在整整十年间是该地区的祸患。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>boorish</b></td><td>/ˈbʊrɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁无礼的</td><td>boorish behavior<br>粗鲁无礼的举动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His boorish behavior at the table embarrassed everyone present.  他在餐桌上粗鲁无礼的举动让在场所有人难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 churlish</td><td>/ˈtʃɜːrlɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁无礼的</td><td>a churlish refusal<br>无礼的拒绝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be churlish to refuse such a generous gift.  拒绝如此丰厚的礼物就太无礼了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 classless</td><td>/ˈklæsləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗俗的，没有教养的</td><td>a classless remark<br>一句粗俗的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Mocking the waiter was a classless thing to do.  取笑服务员是很没教养的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loutish</td><td>/ˈlaʊtɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁的</td><td>loutish manners<br>粗鲁的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His loutish manners at the dinner table embarrassed his entire family.  他在餐桌上粗鲁的举止让全家人都很难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uncouth</td><td>/ʌnˈkuːθ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁的，无教养的</td><td>uncouth table manners<br>粗野的餐桌举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His uncouth remarks at dinner offended nearly everyone at the table.  他在晚餐上粗鲁的言辞几乎惹恼了同桌所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 couth</del></td><td>/kuːθ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，文雅的</td><td>a couth young man<br>一个有教养的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was surprisingly couth for someone raised on the streets.  对一个街头长大的人来说，他出奇地有教养。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cultivated</td><td>/ˈkʌltɪveɪtɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，有修养的</td><td>a cultivated taste in art<br>高雅的艺术品位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her cultivated manners impressed everyone at the dinner.  她优雅的举止让晚宴上的每个人都印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 polished</td><td>/ˈpɑːlɪʃt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的</td><td>polished manners<br>优雅得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His polished manners impressed everyone at the formal dinner.  他优雅得体的举止让正式晚宴上的每个人都印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 refined</td><td>/rəˈfaɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精炼的，纯净的；有教养的</td><td>refined sugar<br>精制糖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Refined sugar has been stripped of every impurity.  精制糖已去除了所有杂质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 well-bred</td><td>/wɛl ˈbrɛd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，有礼貌的</td><td>a well-bred young woman<br>一位有教养的年轻女子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The well-bred guest thanked the host before leaving.  这位有教养的客人离开前向主人道了谢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 courteous</td><td>/ˈkɜːrtiəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>礼貌的，谦恭有礼的</td><td>a courteous reply<br>礼貌的答复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even when customers shouted, the young clerk remained calm and courteous.  即使顾客大声嚷嚷，那位年轻店员仍然平静而有礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 polite</td><td>/pəˈlaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有礼貌的，客气的</td><td>make polite conversation<br>客套地寒暄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is polite to greet your neighbors in the morning.  早上向邻居问好是有礼貌的做法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>bootless</b></del></td><td>/ˈbuːtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无用的</td><td>a bootless effort<br>无用的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Arguing with him about money proved a bootless effort.  和他争论钱的事结果是无用的努力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 barren</td><td>/ˈbærən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；无成果的</td><td>barren land<br>贫瘠的土地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing grows on the barren land beyond the last village.  最后一个村子之外的贫瘠土地上什么都长不出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ineffective</td><td>/ˌɪnɪˈfektɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无效的，不起作用的</td><td>an ineffective treatment<br>无效的治疗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old medicine proved completely ineffective against the new virus.  这种旧药对新病毒完全无效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 futile</td><td>/ˈfjuːtl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无效的，无用的</td><td>a futile attempt<br>徒劳的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trying to persuade him was clearly a futile attempt.  试图说服他显然是徒劳之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abortive</td><td>/əˈbɔːrtɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>失败的，无结果的</td><td>an abortive coup<br>一次失败的政变</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After two abortive attempts, they abandoned the climb.  两次尝试失败后，他们放弃了攀登。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fruitless</td><td>/ˈfruːtləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无成效的，白费的</td><td>a fruitless search<br>毫无结果的搜寻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Weeks of fruitless negotiation left both sides exhausted.  数周毫无结果的谈判让双方都疲惫不堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vain</td><td>/ˈveɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>徒劳的；自负的</td><td>in vain<br>徒劳地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They searched all night in vain for the missing dog.  他们整夜寻找失踪的狗，却是徒劳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 worthy</td><td>/ˈwɜːrði/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得的，有价值的</td><td>a worthy cause<br>一项有价值的事业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The charity is a worthy cause deserving public support.  这家慈善机构值得公众支持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bound</b></td><td>/baʊnd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>界限；决心的，一定会的</td><td>within the bounds of law<br>在法律范围内</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her curiosity knew no bounds when she entered the old library.  她走进那座旧图书馆时，好奇心毫无边界。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 environs</td><td>/ɪnˈvaɪrənz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>周边地区，近郊</td><td>the environs of the city<br>城市周边地区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Farms still cover much of the environs of the old capital.  农田仍覆盖着这座古都的大部分近郊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 limit</td><td>/ˈlɪmət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>界限，限度；限制</td><td>exceed the speed limit<br>超过限速</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was fined for exceeding the speed limit downtown.  他因在市区超速而被罚款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confines</td><td>/ˈkɑːnˌfaɪnz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>范围，界限</td><td>the confines of the campus<br>校园的范围</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students must stay within the confines of the campus.  学生必须待在校园范围内。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perimeter</td><td>/pəˈrɪmɪtər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>边界，界限</td><td>guard the perimeter<br>守卫外围边界</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soldiers guarded the perimeter of the camp throughout the night.  士兵们整夜守卫着营地的周边界线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 resolute</td><td>/ˈrezəluːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定的</td><td>remain resolute<br>保持坚定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Despite heavy criticism, the mayor remained resolute about closing the old factory.  尽管备受批评，市长在关闭旧工厂一事上依然坚定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 determined</td><td>/dɪˈtɜːrmənd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定的，下决心的</td><td>determined to succeed<br>决心成功</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was determined to finish the race despite her injury.  尽管受伤，她仍决心跑完比赛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 single-minded</td><td>/ˈsɪŋɡəl ˈmaɪndɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>一心一意的，专注的</td><td>single-minded devotion to work<br>一心一意投入工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His single-minded pursuit of the record cost him his friendships.  他一心追求纪录，代价是失去了友情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bent on</td><td>/bɛnt ˈɑn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>决心要做…的</td><td>bent on revenge<br>一心想复仇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young officer seemed bent on proving himself.  这位年轻军官似乎决心要证明自己。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unrestrainedness</del></td><td>/ˌʌnrɪˈstreɪndnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无节制，放纵</td><td>the unrestrainedness of youth<br>青春的放纵不羁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unrestrainedness of his spending finally ruined the family.  他毫无节制的花钱最终毁了这个家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 faltering</td><td>/ˈfɔːltərɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>犹豫的；蹒跚的</td><td>a faltering voice<br>犹豫颤抖的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She answered in a faltering voice, unsure of herself.  她声音犹豫地回答，显得没有把握。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hesitant</td><td>/ˈhezɪtənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>犹豫的，迟疑的</td><td>hesitant about the offer<br>对这个提议犹豫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was hesitant about moving to a new city alone.  他对独自搬到新城市感到犹豫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vacillating</td><td>/ˈvæsəˌleɪtɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>摇摆不定的，犹豫的</td><td>a vacillating leader<br>摇摆不定的领导者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company suffered under a vacillating chief executive.  公司在一位摇摆不定的总裁手下吃尽苦头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wavering</td><td>/ˈweɪvərɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>动摇的，游移不定的</td><td>wavering support<br>动摇的支持</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Polls showed the wavering support of rural voters.  民调显示农村选民的支持正在动摇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 weak-kneed</td><td>/wiːk ˈniːd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>软弱的，缺乏决断的</td><td>a weak-kneed compromise<br>软弱的妥协</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics called the deal a weak-kneed surrender to industry.  批评者称这项协议是对行业的软弱屈服。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 6

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>boycott</b></td><td>/ˈbɔɪkɑːt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>联合抵制，拒绝参与</td><td>boycott foreign goods<br>抵制外国商品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Angry customers decided to boycott the store until prices came down.  愤怒的顾客决定抵制这家商店，直到价格下降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 refuse</td><td>/rɪˈfjuːz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>拒绝，不肯；废弃物</td><td>refuse to cooperate<br>拒绝合作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness refused to answer any further questions in court.  证人拒绝在法庭上回答任何进一步的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 patronize</td><td>/ˈpeɪtrənaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞助；以高人一等的态度对待</td><td>patronize the arts<br>赞助艺术</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wealthy merchants patronized young painters throughout the Renaissance.  富商在整个文艺复兴时期都赞助年轻画家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bracing</b></td><td>/ˈbreɪsɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人振奋的，提神的</td><td>a bracing sea breeze<br>令人振奋的海风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A bracing wind off the lake woke us up immediately.  湖面吹来的清冽寒风立刻让我们清醒了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 invigorating</td><td>/ɪnˈvɪɡəreɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人精神焕发的</td><td>an invigorating morning walk<br>令人振奋的晨间散步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A cold shower can be surprisingly invigorating after a run.  跑步后洗个冷水澡出奇地让人精神焕发。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rejuvenating</td><td>/rɪˈdʒuːvəneɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>使恢复活力的</td><td>a rejuvenating holiday<br>让人恢复活力的假期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two weeks by the lake proved wonderfully rejuvenating.  在湖边的两周让人格外恢复了活力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reviving</td><td>/rɪˈvaɪvɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>使苏醒的，令人重振的</td><td>a reviving cup of tea<br>一杯提神的茶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She handed him a reviving cup of strong coffee.  她给他递上一杯提神的浓咖啡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stimulating</td><td>/ˈstɪmjəˌleɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>激励的，令人振奋的</td><td>a stimulating discussion<br>一场激发思考的讨论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The seminar was the most stimulating class of the term.  这场研讨会是本学期最能激发思考的课。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vapid</td><td>/ˈvæpɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无趣的，乏味的</td><td>a vapid conversation<br>乏味的谈话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party ended in vapid conversation about traffic and the weather.  聚会最后只剩下关于交通和天气的乏味闲谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>brake</b></td><td>/breɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>刹车</td><td>brake sharply<br>急刹车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The driver braked hard when a deer crossed the road.  司机看到一只鹿穿过马路，猛地刹了车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decelerate</td><td>/dɪˈseləreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>减速，放慢</td><td>decelerate before the curve<br>转弯前减速</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The train decelerated smoothly as it entered the station.  列车进站时平稳地减了速。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 retard</td><td>/rɪˈtɑːrd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减速，延迟</td><td>retard the growth<br>延缓生长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Language barriers retarded the progress of the peace negotiations.  语言障碍阻碍了和平谈判的进展。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slacken</td><td>/ˈslækən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>放慢，减弱；放松</td><td>slacken the pace<br>放慢速度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The runners slackened their pace on the final hill.  跑者们在最后一个坡上放慢了速度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 accelerate</td><td>/əkˈseləreɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>加速，加快</td><td>accelerate the process<br>加快进程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New funding accelerated the development of the vaccine.  新的资金加快了疫苗的研发。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hasten</td><td>/ˈheɪsn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加速，促进；催促</td><td>hasten the decision<br>促使尽快决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warm weather hastened the melting of snow on the high mountain passes.  暖和的天气促进了高山山口积雪的融化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rush</td><td>/rʌʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>匆忙，急速行动；催促</td><td>rush to the airport<br>赶往机场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They rushed to the airport and barely caught the flight.  他们赶往机场，勉强赶上了航班。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 speed up</td><td>/spiːd ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>加速，加快</td><td>speed up production<br>加快生产</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The factory sped up production to meet holiday demand.  工厂加快生产以满足假期需求。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>brash</b></td><td>/bræʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚勇的，鲁莽的</td><td>a brash young executive<br>鲁莽的年轻主管</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His brash remarks offended nearly everyone at the dinner table.  他鲁莽的言论几乎冒犯了餐桌上的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 audacious</td><td>/ɔːˈdeɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，（但往往）愚勇的；令人吃惊的</td><td>an audacious plan<br>大胆的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general approved an audacious plan to cross the river at night.  将军批准了一个夜间渡河的大胆计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brassy</td><td>/ˈbræsɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>厚脸皮的，不知羞耻的</td><td>a brassy manner<br>厚脸皮的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her brassy confidence annoyed the older members of the committee.  她那种不知羞耻的自信惹恼了委员会的老成员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 daredevil</td><td>/ˈderdevl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>大胆鲁莽的（人）</td><td>a daredevil stunt<br>一次鲁莽的特技</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The daredevil rider jumped his motorcycle over six parked cars.  那位鲁莽大胆的骑手驾摩托车跃过六辆停放的汽车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 madcap</td><td>/ˈmædkæp/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>莽撞冲动的；莽撞冲动的人</td><td>a madcap adventure<br>一场冒险的疯狂之旅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a madcap would try to cross that river during the flood.  只有热爱冒险的疯子才会在洪水期试图渡过那条河。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reckless</td><td>/ˈrekləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不考虑后果的，大胆鲁莽的</td><td>reckless driving<br>鲁莽驾驶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His reckless driving on the icy road caused a serious accident.  他在结冰路面上的鲁莽驾驶造成了一场严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 temerarious</del></td><td>/ˌtɛməˈrɛriəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>鲁莽的，胆大妄为的</td><td>a temerarious decision<br>一个鲁莽的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was temerarious of him to sail alone in that storm.  他在那场风暴中独自出海实在鲁莽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 circumspect</td><td>/ˈsɜːrkəmspekt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，小心的</td><td>remain circumspect in public<br>在公开场合保持谨慎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Diplomats stay circumspect in public and blunt behind closed doors.  外交官在公开场合谨慎，关起门来则直言不讳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 guarded</td><td>/ˈɡɑːrdəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，有保留的</td><td>a guarded response<br>有保留的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister gave a guarded welcome to the new proposal.  部长对新提案表示了有保留的欢迎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 heedful</del></td><td>/ˈhiːdfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>留心的，注意的</td><td>heedful of the warning<br>留意警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heedful of the forecast, the climbers turned back early.  留意到天气预报，登山者提早返回了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prudent</td><td>/ˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的；小心谨慎的，审慎的</td><td>a prudent investment<br>一笔审慎的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be prudent to save part of every paycheck.  每份工资都存下一部分才是明智之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wary</td><td>/ˈweri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>警惕的，谨慎的</td><td>wary of strangers<br>对陌生人警惕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children are taught to be wary of strangers offering free rides.  孩子们被教导要警惕主动提供搭车的陌生人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>brassy</b></td><td>/ˈbræsɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>厚脸皮的，不知羞耻的</td><td>a brassy manner<br>厚脸皮的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her brassy confidence annoyed the older members of the committee.  她那种不知羞耻的自信惹恼了委员会的老成员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 audacious</td><td>/ɔːˈdeɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，（但往往）愚勇的；令人吃惊的</td><td>an audacious plan<br>大胆的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general approved an audacious plan to cross the river at night.  将军批准了一个夜间渡河的大胆计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bold-faced</td><td>/boʊld ˈfeɪst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无耻的，公然大胆的</td><td>a bold-faced lie<br>一个无耻的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He told a bold-faced lie without even blushing.  他撒了个无耻的谎，脸都不红。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brazen</td><td>/ˈbreɪzn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>蛮横大胆的，厚颜无耻的；大胆自信地去面对或从事</td><td>a brazen lie<br>无耻的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She told a brazen lie without even lowering her eyes.  她说了个无耻的谎言，连眼睛都没垂下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impertinent</td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrtnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不相关的；无礼的，鲁莽的</td><td>an impertinent remark<br>无礼的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected that the question was impertinent to the charges.  律师反对说，这个问题与指控无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impudent</td><td>/ˈɪmpjədənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>放肆大胆的，无礼的</td><td>an impudent remark<br>一句无礼的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The impudent student argued with the professor in class.  这个无礼的学生在课上与教授争辩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insolent</td><td>/ˈɪnsələnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗野的，无礼的</td><td>an insolent tone<br>无礼的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The student's insolent reply to the teacher shocked the entire classroom.  这名学生对老师无礼的回答震惊了整个教室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 diffident</td><td>/ˈdɪfɪdənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏自信的，羞怯的</td><td>a diffident newcomer<br>腼腆的新人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was too diffident to argue with his famous professor.  他太拘谨，不敢与那位名教授争辩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unassertive</del></td><td>/ˌʌnəˈsɜrtɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不自信的，不敢主张的</td><td>too unassertive to complain<br>太腼腆而不敢抱怨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was too unassertive to ask for the raise she deserved.  她太不敢主张，没去要她应得的加薪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 retiring</td><td>/rɪˈtaɪrɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>腼腆的，不爱交际的</td><td>a shy and retiring man<br>一个害羞内敛的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The author was a retiring man who avoided interviews.  这位作家性格内敛，从不接受采访。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 timid</td><td>/ˈtɪmɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆怯的，羞怯的</td><td>a timid smile<br>怯生生的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a timid smile and slipped into the back row.  她怯生生地一笑，溜进后排座位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bravado</b></td><td>/brəˈvɑːdoʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>虚张声势</td><td>an act of bravado<br>一次虚张声势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His loud threats were pure bravado, and nobody took them seriously.  他大声的威胁纯属虚张声势，没人当真。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bravura</b></td><td>/brəˈvjʊrə/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>精湛华丽的；炫技表演</td><td>a bravura performance<br>精彩绝伦的演出</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The violinist closed the concert with a stunning bravura solo.  小提琴手以一段炫技独奏为音乐会收尾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adroit</td><td>/əˈdrɔɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟练的，灵巧的</td><td>adroit at negotiation<br>擅长谈判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She proved remarkably adroit at handling hostile questions from reporters.  她在应对记者的敌意提问时表现得非常娴熟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 artful</td><td>/ˈɑːrtfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>技艺高超的；狡猾的</td><td>an artful arrangement<br>巧妙的布置</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chef's artful plating made the dish look like a painting.  厨师巧妙的摆盘让这道菜看起来像幅画。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dexterous</td><td>/ˈdekstrəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>动作灵活的；头脑灵活的</td><td>dexterous fingers<br>灵巧的手指</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The surgeon's dexterous hands worked steadily for more than six hours.  这位外科医生灵活的双手稳稳地工作了六个多小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 masterful</td><td>/ˈmæstərfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>技艺精湛的，娴熟的</td><td>a masterful performance<br>一场技艺精湛的演出</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her masterful handling of the violin silenced the whole hall.  她精湛的小提琴技艺让全场肃静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 amateur</td><td>/ˈæməˌtɜːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>业余爱好者；外行的</td><td>a talented amateur<br>有天赋的业余者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comet was first spotted by an amateur astronomer.  这颗彗星最先由一位业余天文学家发现。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 artless</td><td>/ˈɑrtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏技巧的，笨拙的</td><td>an artless imitation<br>拙劣的模仿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting was an artless copy of a famous portrait.  这幅画是对一幅名作的拙劣临摹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unprofessional</td><td>/ˌʌnprəˈfeʃənəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不专业的，外行的</td><td>unprofessional conduct<br>不专业的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Arriving late to every meeting looks deeply unprofessional.  每次会议都迟到显得极不专业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unskillful</td><td>/ʌnˈskɪlfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不熟练的，技艺拙劣的</td><td>unskillful workmanship<br>拙劣的手工活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unskillful repair left the table wobbling badly.  这次拙劣的修理让桌子摇晃得厉害。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>brazen</b></td><td>/ˈbreɪzn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>蛮横大胆的，厚颜无耻的；大胆自信地去面对或从事</td><td>a brazen lie<br>无耻的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She told a brazen lie without even lowering her eyes.  她说了个无耻的谎言，连眼睛都没垂下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bold-faced</td><td>/boʊld ˈfeɪst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无耻的，公然大胆的</td><td>a bold-faced lie<br>一个无耻的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He told a bold-faced lie without even blushing.  他撒了个无耻的谎，脸都不红。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impertinent</td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrtnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不相关的；无礼的，鲁莽的</td><td>an impertinent remark<br>无礼的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected that the question was impertinent to the charges.  律师反对说，这个问题与指控无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impudent</td><td>/ˈɪmpjədənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>放肆大胆的，无礼的</td><td>an impudent remark<br>一句无礼的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The impudent student argued with the professor in class.  这个无礼的学生在课上与教授争辩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insolent</td><td>/ˈɪnsələnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗野的，无礼的</td><td>an insolent tone<br>无礼的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The student's insolent reply to the teacher shocked the entire classroom.  这名学生对老师无礼的回答震惊了整个教室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confront</td><td>/kənˈfrʌnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>直接对抗，直面</td><td>confront the problem<br>直面问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sooner or later you must confront the problem directly.  你早晚必须直面这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 outface</del></td><td>/aʊtˈfeɪs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>以气势压倒，公然对抗</td><td>outface his accusers<br>气势压倒指控者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness outfaced the lawyer without dropping her gaze.  证人目光丝毫不移，气势压过了律师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 defy</td><td>/dɪˈfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>蔑视，公然违抗</td><td>defy an order<br>违抗命令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thousands of workers defied the ban and gathered in the main square.  数千名工人公然违抗禁令，聚集在中心广场上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modest</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谦虚的；不大的，适度的</td><td>modest about her success<br>对成功很谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed modest about his award and thanked his team.  他对自己的奖项很谦虚，还感谢了团队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 self-effacing</td><td>/sɛlf ɪˈfeɪsɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不爱出头的，谦逊低调的</td><td>a self-effacing scientist<br>一位低调谦逊的科学家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The self-effacing author refused to appear on television.  这位低调的作家拒绝上电视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 diffident</td><td>/ˈdɪfɪdənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏自信的，羞怯的</td><td>a diffident newcomer<br>腼腆的新人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was too diffident to argue with his famous professor.  他太拘谨，不敢与那位名教授争辩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 retiring</td><td>/rɪˈtaɪrɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>腼腆的，不爱交际的</td><td>a shy and retiring man<br>一个害羞内敛的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The author was a retiring man who avoided interviews.  这位作家性格内敛，从不接受采访。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 timid</td><td>/ˈtɪmɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆怯的，羞怯的</td><td>a timid smile<br>怯生生的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a timid smile and slipped into the back row.  她怯生生地一笑，溜进后排座位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dodge</td><td>/dɑːdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>躲避</td><td>dodge the question<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister managed to dodge the question three times in one interview.  部长在一次采访中三次成功回避了这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 duck</td><td>/dʌk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>躲避，回避；迅速低头</td><td>duck the question<br>回避这个问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister ducked every question about the budget.  部长回避了所有关于预算的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 shirk</td><td>/ʃɜːrk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>逃避，规避</td><td>shirk responsibility<br>逃避责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one on this team is allowed to shirk hard work.  这个团队里没人可以逃避苦活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sidestep</td><td>/ˈsaɪdstep/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>回避，避开（问题）</td><td>sidestep the issue<br>回避这个问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The candidate neatly sidestepped the issue of rising unemployment.  这位候选人巧妙地回避了失业率上升的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>breach</b></td><td>/briːtʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>违背</td><td>breach the contract<br>违约</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company breached the contract by delivering the goods late.  这家公司延迟交货，违反了合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contravene</td><td>/ˌkɑːntrəˈviːn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>违反，反对</td><td>contravene the rules<br>违反规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Building a factory here would contravene several local environmental regulations.  在此建厂会违反数条当地环保法规。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fracture</td><td>/ˈfræktʃər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>破裂</td><td>a hairline fracture<br>一道细微裂缝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scan revealed a hairline fracture in his left wrist.  扫描显示他左手腕有一道细微裂缝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 infringe</td><td>/ɪnˈfrɪndʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>违反；侵犯（权利）</td><td>infringe on his rights<br>侵犯他的权利</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new rule infringes on the privacy of every employee.  新规定侵犯了每位员工的隐私。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transgress</td><td>/trænzˈɡres/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>违背；犯错</td><td>transgress the rules<br>违反规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Any player who transgresses the rules will be removed from the tournament.  任何违反规则的选手都将被逐出这场比赛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 obey</td><td>/oʊˈbeɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>服从，遵守</td><td>obey the rules<br>遵守规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Drivers must obey the speed limit in school zones.  司机在学校区域必须遵守限速。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 observe</td><td>/əbˈzɜːrv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>遵守；观察</td><td>observe the law<br>遵守法律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 All visitors are expected to observe the museum's rules.  所有参观者都应遵守博物馆的规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 comply with</td><td>/kəmˈplaɪ wɪð/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>遵守，服从</td><td>comply with regulations<br>遵守规章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every factory must comply with the new emission standards.  每家工厂都必须遵守新的排放标准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conform to</td><td>/kənˈfɔrm tuː/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>遵守，符合</td><td>conform to the standard<br>符合标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every part must conform to the national safety standard.  每个零件都必须符合国家安全标准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>brevity</b></td><td>/ˈbrevəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>简短，简洁</td><td>for the sake of brevity<br>为了简洁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 For the sake of brevity, I will skip the technical details.  为了简洁，我会略过技术细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 briefness</del></td><td>/ˈbriːfnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>简短，短暂</td><td>the briefness of the visit<br>这次来访的短暂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The briefness of his reply suggested he was annoyed.  他回答之简短说明他有些恼火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conciseness</td><td>/kənˈsaɪsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>简洁，简明</td><td>praised for its conciseness<br>因简洁而受赞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Editors valued the report for its conciseness and clarity.  编辑们赞赏这份报告简洁清晰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shortness</td><td>/ˈʃɔːrtnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>短，简短</td><td>the shortness of the notice<br>通知时间之短</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The shortness of the deadline forced them to work overnight.  截止期限太短，逼得他们通宵赶工。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 lengthiness</del></td><td>/ˈlɛŋθinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冗长，啰嗦</td><td>the lengthiness of the speech<br>演讲的冗长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Readers complained about the lengthiness of the introduction.  读者抱怨引言太过冗长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 7

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bribe</b></td><td>/braɪb/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>贿赂，收买</td><td>bribe an official<br>贿赂官员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to bribe the guard with a thick envelope of cash.  他试图用一叠现金贿赂那名警卫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 corrupt</td><td>/kəˈrʌpt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>腐败的；使腐化，收买</td><td>a corrupt official<br>一名腐败官员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The corrupt inspector accepted cash to ignore the violations.  这名腐败的检查员收现金来无视违规。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pay off</td><td>/peɪ ˈɔf/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>收买，贿赂；还清</td><td>pay off the inspector<br>收买检查员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They tried to pay off the inspector to avoid a fine.  他们试图收买检查员以逃避罚款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 square</td><td>/ˈskwer/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>贿赂，收买；使一致</td><td>square the local officials<br>把当地官员收买好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The developer had squared the local officials long before construction.  开发商早在动工前就把当地官员收买好了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bridle</b></td><td>/ˈbraɪdl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>限制</td><td>bridle his anger<br>克制他的怒气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She bridled her temper and answered the rude question politely.  她克制住脾气，礼貌地回答了那个无礼的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 check</td><td>/tʃek/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使突然停止，阻止；检查，核对</td><td>check the spread of disease<br>阻止疾病蔓延</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Strict quarantine measures checked the spread of the disease.  严格的隔离措施阻止了疾病的蔓延。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contain</td><td>/kənˈteɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>控制，抑制；包含</td><td>contain the outbreak<br>控制疫情爆发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors worked around the clock to contain the outbreak.  医生们昼夜不停地努力控制疫情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curb</td><td>/ˈkɜːrb/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>控制，约束</td><td>curb government spending<br>控制政府开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city introduced new rules to curb traffic downtown.  市政府出台新规以限制市区交通。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 constrain</td><td>/kənˈstreɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>限制</td><td>constrain his freedom<br>限制他的自由</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Strict rules constrain his freedom to travel outside the country.  严格的规定限制了他出国旅行的自由。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inhibit</td><td>/ɪnˈhɪbɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抑制，阻碍</td><td>inhibit the growth of bacteria<br>抑制细菌生长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cold weather inhibits the growth of these tropical plants.  寒冷天气抑制了这些热带植物的生长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 regulate</td><td>/ˈreɡjəˌleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>管理，调控，规范</td><td>regulate the industry<br>对该行业进行监管</td></tr>
