<tr><td colspan="7">例 New laws regulate how companies collect personal data.  新法规范了公司收集个人数据的方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 restrain</td><td>/rɪˈstreɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抑制，约束，制止</td><td>restrain his temper<br>克制他的脾气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She could hardly restrain her laughter during the speech.  演讲时她几乎忍不住笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tame</td><td>/teɪm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>被驯化的；控制，抑制</td><td>tame the wild horse<br>驯服野马</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took the trainer months to tame the wild horse completely.  训马师花了几个月才彻底驯服那匹野马。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rein in</td><td>/reɪn ˈɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>控制，约束</td><td>rein in spending<br>控制开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board reined in spending after two bad quarters.  两个季度业绩不佳后，董事会控制了开支。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>brisk</b></td><td>/brɪsk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满生机的，有活力的</td><td>a brisk walk<br>快步散步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A brisk walk before breakfast leaves me alert all morning.  早餐前快走一趟能让我整个上午都很清醒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 animated</td><td>/ˈænəˌmeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>活跃的，生气勃勃的</td><td>an animated discussion<br>一场热烈的讨论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The class turned into an animated debate about ethics.  这堂课变成了一场关于伦理的热烈辩论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bouncing</td><td>/ˈbaʊnsɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>活泼健康的，精力充沛的</td><td>a bouncing baby boy<br>健康活泼的男婴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave birth to a bouncing baby boy last spring.  去年春天她生下一个健康活泼的男婴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bustling</td><td>/ˈbʌslɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熙熙攘攘的，忙碌的</td><td>a bustling market<br>熙熙攘攘的集市</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The port grew into a bustling center of trade.  这座港口发展成了繁忙的贸易中心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frisky</td><td>/ˈfrɪski/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>活泼欢腾的</td><td>a frisky puppy<br>一只活泼的小狗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The frisky colt raced across the field at dawn.  黎明时那匹活泼的小马驹在田野上奔跑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 kinetic</td><td>/kəˈnetɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满活力的；运动的</td><td>kinetic energy<br>动能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dancer's kinetic style filled the stage with motion.  这位舞者充满动感的风格让舞台生机勃勃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sprightly</td><td>/ˈspraɪtli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>活泼的，充满活力的</td><td>a sprightly old man<br>一位活泼的老人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sprightly old man led the hikers up the steep mountain path.  一位活泼的老人带着徒步者爬上陡峭的山路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vibrant</td><td>/ˈvaɪbrənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满活力的；鲜艳的</td><td>a vibrant city<br>一座充满活力的城市</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The neighborhood became a vibrant hub for young artists.  这个街区成了年轻艺术家的活力中心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lackadaisical</td><td>/ˌlækəˈdeɪzɪkəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>懒散的，无兴趣的</td><td>a lackadaisical attitude<br>懒散的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lackadaisical attitude toward homework worried both parents.  他对作业懒散的态度让父母都很担心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 languid</td><td>/ˈlæŋɡwɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没精打采的，虚弱的</td><td>a languid summer afternoon<br>慢悠悠的夏日午后</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A languid summer afternoon passed with nobody willing to leave the shade.  一个慢悠悠的夏日午后过去了，没人愿意离开树荫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 leaden</td><td>/ˈledən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉重的，无生气的；铅灰的</td><td>a leaden sky<br>铅灰色的天空</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He walked home with leaden steps after the painful defeat.  痛苦的失败后，他步履沉重地走回家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dead</td><td>/ˈded/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>死的；毫无生气的</td><td>a dead town at night<br>夜里死寂的小镇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The resort is completely dead in the winter months.  这个度假地在冬季几个月里毫无生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inactive</td><td>/ɪnˈæktɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不活动的，不活跃的</td><td>remain inactive for months<br>数月毫无动作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The volcano has been inactive for over two hundred years.  这座火山已有两百多年没有活动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lifeless</td><td>/ˈlaɪfləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无生命的；无生气的</td><td>a lifeless performance<br>毫无生气的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The actors gave a lifeless performance to a bored audience.  演员们在无聊的观众前演得毫无生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bristle</b></td><td>/ˈbrɪsl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>愤然发怒，恼怒</td><td>bristle at the suggestion<br>对该提议大为不满</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He bristled at the suggestion that his research was careless.  有人说他的研究草率，他顿时怒不可遏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bluster</td><td>/ˈblʌstər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>狂妄自大地大声说；喧闹的状态</td><td>bluster and threaten<br>大声吓唬威胁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager began to bluster and threaten when questioned closely.  被仔细追问时，经理开始狂妄自大地大声吓唬威胁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fulminate</td><td>/ˈfʌlmɪneɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>大声斥责</td><td>fulminate against corruption<br>大声斥责腐败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old editor fulminated against corruption in every weekly column.  那位老编辑在每周的专栏里大声斥责腐败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rampage</td><td>/ˈræmˌpeɪdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>横冲直撞，暴怒乱闯</td><td>go on a rampage<br>大肆撒野闹事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Elephants rampaged through the village, destroying several huts.  大象横冲直撞穿过村子，毁了几间棚屋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fume</td><td>/ˈfjuːm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>发怒，气恼；冒烟</td><td>fume over the delay<br>为延误而气恼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sat fuming in the car after the meeting was canceled.  会议取消后他坐在车里生闷气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 storm</td><td>/ˈstɔːrm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>暴风雨；一阵猛烈（如抗议）</td><td>a storm of protest<br>一阵抗议风暴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The decision caused a storm of protest on social media.  这一决定在社交媒体上引发抗议风暴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cower</td><td>/ˈkaʊər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>畏缩</td><td>cower in the corner<br>在角落里畏缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The frightened puppy cowered under the table during the loud thunderstorm.  雷雨大作时，惊恐的小狗畏缩在桌子下面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>brittle</b></td><td>/ˈbrɪtl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易碎的，脆弱的；冷淡的，不真心的</td><td>brittle old paper<br>脆化的旧纸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The brittle old pages cracked when I turned them carefully.  我小心翻动时，那些脆化的旧书页还是裂开了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crispy</td><td>/ˈkrɪspi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>脆的，酥脆的</td><td>crispy fried chicken<br>酥脆的炸鸡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crispy skin of the roast duck cracked at the touch.  烤鸭酥脆的皮一碰就裂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crumbly</td><td>/ˈkrʌmbli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易碎成屑的，松散的</td><td>crumbly cheese<br>易碎的奶酪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soil here is dry and crumbly between the fingers.  这里的土干燥松散，一捏就散。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flaky</td><td>/ˈfleɪki/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易剥落成片的；不可靠的</td><td>flaky pastry<br>层层酥脆的点心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old paint on the door was dry and flaky.  门上的旧漆干裂，一片片剥落。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 friable</td><td>/ˈfraɪəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易碎的</td><td>friable dry soil<br>易碎的干土</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The friable dry soil crumbled easily between his rough fingers.  易碎的干土在他粗糙的手指间轻易碎裂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chilly</td><td>/ˈtʃɪli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>寒冷的；冷淡的</td><td>a chilly reception<br>冷淡的接待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The proposal met a chilly reception from the committee.  这项提议受到委员会的冷淡对待。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frigid</td><td>/ˈfrɪdʒɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严寒的；冷漠的</td><td>a frigid stare<br>冷冰冰的凝视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave the latecomer a frigid stare and said nothing.  她冷冰冰地盯了迟到者一眼，什么也没说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frosty</td><td>/ˈfrɔːsti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>寒冷的；无强烈感情的，冷淡的</td><td>a frosty welcome<br>冷淡的欢迎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The visitors received a frosty welcome from the village elders.  来访者受到村中长者冷淡的接待。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glacial</td><td>/ˈɡleɪsiəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极冷的；冷漠的，无感情的</td><td>a glacial silence<br>冰冷的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His glacial tone made everyone at the table stop talking at once.  他冰冷的语气让桌边所有人立刻停止了交谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unfriendly</td><td>/ʌnˈfrendli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不友好的，冷淡的</td><td>an unfriendly stare<br>不友好的注视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The waiter gave us an unfriendly look and walked away.  服务员不友好地看了我们一眼便走开了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unsympathetic</td><td>/ˌʌnsɪmpəˈθetɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不同情的，冷漠的</td><td>an unsympathetic audience<br>冷漠的听众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager was unsympathetic to complaints about long hours.  经理对加班时间过长的抱怨毫无同情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cordial</td><td>/ˈkɔːrdʒəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情友好的；令人振奋的</td><td>a cordial welcome<br>热情的欢迎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two leaders exchanged cordial greetings before the talks.  两位领导人在会谈前互致亲切的问候。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 genial</td><td>/ˈdʒiːniəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和蔼亲切的；温和宜人的</td><td>a genial host<br>亲切的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our genial neighbor invited the whole street over for dinner.  我们和蔼可亲的邻居请整条街的人来吃晚饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 warmhearted</td><td>/ˈwɔrm ˈhɑrtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热心的，热情友善的</td><td>a warmhearted neighbor<br>一位热心的邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their warmhearted host insisted that everyone stay for dinner.  他们热心的主人坚持要所有人留下吃饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>broach</b></td><td>/broʊtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提出讨论</td><td>broach the subject<br>提出这个话题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nobody dared to broach the topic of money at the reunion.  聚会上没人敢提出钱的话题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moot</td><td>/ˈmuːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>提出讨论；有争议的</td><td>moot a new proposal<br>提出新提议供讨论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several members mooted the idea of moving the deadline.  几名成员提出了推迟截止日期的想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 place</td><td>/ˈpleɪs/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提出（问题）；放置</td><td>place the issue before them<br>把问题提交给他们</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She placed the question before the committee that morning.  那天上午她把这个问题提交给了委员会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 raise</td><td>/ˈreɪz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提出（问题）；举起，提高</td><td>raise an objection<br>提出反对意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He raised an objection just before the vote was taken.  就在投票前他提出了反对意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bring up</td><td>/brɪŋ ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>提出（话题）；养育</td><td>bring up the topic<br>提起这个话题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nobody dared bring up the topic of money at dinner.  晚饭时没人敢提起钱的话题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bromide</b></td><td>/ˈbroʊmaɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调；镇静剂</td><td>a tired old bromide<br>老掉牙的陈词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech ended with a tired old bromide about hard work.  他的演讲以一句关于努力工作的陈腐套话作结。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 banality</td><td>/bəˈnæləti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>平庸；陈腐的话</td><td>the banality of his speech<br>他讲话的平庸乏味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film offered nothing but banalities about growing up.  这部电影只提供了些关于成长的陈腐说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cliché</del></td><td>/kliːˈʃeɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调，老套的说法</td><td>an overused cliché<br>用滥的陈词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Saying that time heals everything has become a tired cliché.  说时间治愈一切已成为老套的陈词滥调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 platitude</td><td>/ˈplætɪtuːd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调</td><td>utter a platitude<br>说一句陈词滥调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech offered nothing but platitudes about hard work and dreams.  这篇演讲除了关于努力与梦想的陈词滥调外别无他物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 homily</td><td>/ˈhɑːməli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冗长乏味的道德讲演或训诫</td><td>deliver a homily<br>作一番训诫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We had to sit through another homily about punctuality and respect.  我们只得又听了一通关于守时与尊重的冗长训诫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 truism</td><td>/ˈtruːɪzəm/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调</td><td>a familiar truism<br>一句熟悉的老话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is a truism that hard work alone rarely guarantees success.  仅靠努力很少能保证成功，这是句老生常谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chestnut</td><td>/ˈtʃesnʌt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>老掉牙的话；栗子</td><td>an old chestnut<br>老掉牙的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He repeated that old chestnut about hard work and luck.  他又重复了那句关于努力与运气的老话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shibboleth</td><td>/ˈʃɪbəˌleθ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>陈腐的口号；标志性用语</td><td>an outworn shibboleth<br>过时的老口号</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Free trade became a shibboleth repeated by every candidate.  自由贸易成了每位候选人都挂在嘴上的口号。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>brook</b></td><td>/brʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忍受，容许</td><td>brook no delay<br>不容拖延</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strict coach will brook no excuses for missing practice.  这位严厉的教练不容许任何缺席训练的借口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abide</td><td>/əˈbaɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>容忍；遵守；持续</td><td>cannot abide dishonesty<br>不能容忍不诚实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She cannot abide people who interrupt her while reading.  她无法容忍别人在她读书时打扰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 countenance</td><td>/ˈkaʊntənəns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>容忍；赞成，推崇</td><td>countenance such behavior<br>容忍这种行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The headmaster would not countenance cheating of any kind in his school.  校长绝不容忍校内任何形式的作弊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 endure</td><td>/ɪnˈdʊr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>忍受；持续存在</td><td>endure great hardship<br>忍受巨大艰苦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The settlers endured three winters without proper shelter.  移民们在没有像样住所的情况下熬过了三个冬天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stomach</td><td>/ˈstʌmək/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>容忍</td><td>cannot stomach the insult<br>无法忍受这种侮辱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He could not stomach the insult and walked out of the meeting.  他无法忍受这种侮辱，径直退出会议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>browbeat</b></td><td>/ˈbraʊbiːt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恐吓</td><td>browbeat the witness<br>恐吓证人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer tried to browbeat the witness into changing her story.  律师试图恐吓证人改口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blackjack</td><td>/ˈblækˌdʒæk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>以威胁手段强迫；短棍</td><td>blackjack him into silence<br>威逼他保持沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gang blackjacked shopkeepers into paying protection money.  这伙人威逼店主交保护费。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bulldoze</td><td>/ˈbʊlˌdoʊz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强行逼迫；用推土机推平</td><td>bulldoze him into agreeing<br>硬逼他同意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chairman bulldozed the plan through the meeting.  主席强行让这个方案在会上通过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bully</td><td>/ˈbʊli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>欺凌弱小者，恶霸</td><td>stand up to a bully<br>勇敢面对恶霸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new teacher quickly stopped the bully who terrorized younger children.  新老师很快制止了那个欺负小同学的恶霸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cow</td><td>/kaʊ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>恐吓，威胁</td><td>cow them into obedience<br>威吓他们服从</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sergeant's shouting failed to cow the new recruits into silence.  中士的吼叫没能把新兵威吓得沉默下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hector</td><td>/ˈhektər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>威吓，欺凌恫吓</td><td>hector the staff<br>恫吓下属</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach hectored his players throughout the second half.  整个下半场教练都在对球员大声恫吓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intimidate</td><td>/ɪnˈtɪmɪdeɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>威吓</td><td>intimidate the witness<br>威吓证人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The defendant's friends tried to intimidate the witness before the trial began.  被告的朋友试图在审判开始前威吓证人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bruit</b></td><td>/ˈbruːt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（未经证实地）散播（消息）</td><td>bruit the rumor about<br>四处散播谣言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scandal was bruited about long before any facts emerged.  丑闻在任何事实浮现之前就已四处传开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 circulate</td><td>/ˈsɜːrkjəˌleɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>散布，传播；流通</td><td>circulate a rumor<br>散播谣言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rumors of a merger circulated quickly among the staff.  合并的传言在员工中迅速传开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 whisper</td><td>/ˈwɪspər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>低声说，耳语</td><td>whisper in her ear<br>在她耳边低语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She whispered the answer so the teacher would not hear.  她低声说出答案，以免老师听见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 noise about</td><td>/nɔɪz əˈbaʊt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>到处散播（消息）</td><td>noise the story about<br>把这事到处传开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone noised it about that the factory would close.  有人四处传言说工厂要关门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 keep secret</td><td>/kiːp ˈsiːkrət/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>保守秘密，隐瞒</td><td>keep the plan secret<br>把计划保密</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They kept the engagement secret from their families for months.  他们对家人隐瞒了订婚的事好几个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 8

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>buck</b></td><td>/bʌk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>阻止，反对</td><td>buck the trend<br>逆势而行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A few senators bucked the party line and voted against it.  几位参议员违抗党内立场，投了反对票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 defy</td><td>/dɪˈfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>蔑视，公然违抗</td><td>defy an order<br>违抗命令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thousands of workers defied the ban and gathered in the main square.  数千名工人公然违抗禁令，聚集在中心广场上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fight</td><td>/ˈfaɪt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>对抗，斗争；打斗</td><td>fight the proposal<br>反对这项提案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The residents will fight the proposal to widen the old highway.  居民们将反对拓宽这条旧公路的提案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 oppose</td><td>/əˈpoʊz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>反对，抵制</td><td>oppose the new law<br>反对新法律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two senators openly opposed the new tax bill.  两位参议员公开反对这项新税案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repel</td><td>/rɪˈpel/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>击退，驱退；使厌恶</td><td>repel the attackers<br>击退进攻者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The greasy smell of the kitchen seemed to repel every customer who entered.  厨房油腻的气味似乎让每个进来的顾客都感到厌恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 withstand</td><td>/wɪθˈstænd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抵挡，承受得住</td><td>withstand the pressure<br>承受住压力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bridge was built to withstand hurricane winds.  这座桥的建造能抵挡飓风。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assent to</td><td>/əˈsɛnt tuː/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>同意，赞成</td><td>assent to the terms<br>同意这些条款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board finally assented to the revised contract.  董事会最终同意了修改后的合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bow to</td><td>/ˈbaʊ tu/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>屈从于，顺从</td><td>bow to public pressure<br>屈从于公众压力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The government finally bowed to pressure and cancelled the new tax.  政府最终屈从于压力，取消了新税。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 submit to</td><td>/səbˈmɪt tu/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>服从，顺从于</td><td>submit to authority<br>服从权威</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He refused to submit to the will of the majority.  他拒绝服从多数人的意愿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 succumb to</td><td>/səˈkʌm tu/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>屈服于；死于</td><td>succumb to pressure<br>屈服于压力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After weeks of resistance she succumbed to their demands.  抵抗数周后，她屈服于他们的要求。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 surrender to</td><td>/səˈrendər tu/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>向…投降，屈服于</td><td>surrender to the police<br>向警方投降</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rebels finally surrendered to government forces.  叛军最终向政府军投降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 yield to</td><td>/ˈjiːld tu/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>屈服于，让位于</td><td>yield to temptation<br>经不住诱惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She would not yield to threats of any kind.  她不会屈服于任何形式的威胁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>budge</b></td><td>/bʌdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>挪动，微动；改变主意</td><td>refuse to budge<br>拒不让步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heavy stone would not budge despite our best efforts.  尽管我们竭尽全力，那块沉重的石头还是挪不动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 concede</td><td>/kənˈsiːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>承认</td><td>concede the point<br>承认这一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She finally conceded that her opponent had made a fair point.  她最终承认对手说得有道理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relent</td><td>/rɪˈlent/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>变宽容，让步；减弱</td><td>finally relent<br>最终松口让步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her father finally relented and let her borrow the car.  她父亲终于让步，把车借给了她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 submit</td><td>/səbˈmɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>屈服，服从；提交</td><td>submit to authority<br>服从权威</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rebels refused to submit to the new government.  叛军拒绝向新政府屈服。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 succumb</td><td>/səˈkʌm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>屈服；死亡</td><td>succumb to pressure<br>屈服于压力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor finally succumbed to pressure and withdrew the unpopular proposal.  市长最终屈服于压力，撤回了不受欢迎的提案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 surrender</td><td>/səˈrendər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>交出，放弃，投降</td><td>surrender to the police<br>向警方投降</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thief finally surrendered to the police after a long chase.  经过长时间追捕，小偷终于向警方投降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 resist</td><td>/rɪˈzɪst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>抵抗，抗拒，不服从</td><td>resist the temptation<br>抵抗诱惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The villagers resisted every attempt to move them elsewhere.  村民们抵制一切要迁走他们的企图。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bulge</b></td><td>/bʌldʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>凸起</td><td>a bulge in his pocket<br>他口袋里的鼓包</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A strange bulge in his jacket pocket caught the officer's attention.  他夹克口袋里奇怪的鼓包引起了警官的注意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 convexity</td><td>/kənˈvɛksəti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>凸起，凸面</td><td>the convexity of the lens<br>透镜的凸度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The slight convexity of the mirror widens the driver's view.  镜面轻微的凸度扩大了司机的视野。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 projection</td><td>/prəˈdʒekʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>突出物；预测；投影</td><td>a rocky projection<br>一处突出的岩石</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Birds nested on a narrow projection of the cliff face.  鸟在崖壁一处狭窄的突出部筑巢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 protrusion</td><td>/proʊˈtruːʒən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>突出物，隆起</td><td>a bony protrusion<br>一处骨性隆起</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The doctor examined a small protrusion on his wrist.  医生检查了他手腕上一处小突起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 protuberance</td><td>/proʊˈtuːbərəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>隆起物，凸起部分</td><td>a strange protuberance<br>一处奇怪的凸起</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A round protuberance had formed on the trunk of the tree.  树干上长出了一个圆形的凸起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 swell</td><td>/ˈswel/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>膨胀，增大</td><td>swell in numbers<br>数量增加</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd swelled to thousands by early evening.  傍晚时人群增加到了数千人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 depressed region</td><td>/dɪˈprɛst ˈriːdʒən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>凹陷区域；萧条地区</td><td>a broad depressed region<br>一片宽阔的凹陷区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water collected in the depressed region at the field's edge.  水积在田边的凹陷处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cavity</td><td>/ˈkævəti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>洞，凹处；龋洞</td><td>a small tooth cavity<br>一个小龋洞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dentist found a cavity in one of her back teeth.  牙医在她一颗后牙上发现了龋洞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dent</td><td>/dent/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>凹痕，凹陷；使凹陷</td><td>a dent in the door<br>门上的一处凹痕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A small dent in the car door annoyed him for years.  车门上一个小凹痕让他好几年都很在意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indent</td><td>/ɪnˈdent/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>使凹进；缩进（排版）</td><td>indent the first line<br>首行缩进</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please indent the first line of every paragraph.  请把每段的首行缩进。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 recess</td><td>/rɪˈses/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>休会，休庭；课间休息</td><td>take a short recess<br>短暂休会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee will recess for lunch and resume at two.  委员会将休会吃午饭，两点复会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pit</td><td>/ˈpɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>坑，凹陷；果核</td><td>a shallow pit<br>一个浅坑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rain filled the shallow pits left by the machines.  雨水灌满了机器留下的浅坑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bully</b></td><td>/ˈbʊli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>欺凌弱小者，恶霸</td><td>stand up to a bully<br>勇敢面对恶霸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new teacher quickly stopped the bully who terrorized younger children.  新老师很快制止了那个欺负小同学的恶霸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 bullyboy</del></td><td>/ˈbʊliˌbɔɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恶霸，打手</td><td>hired bullyboys<br>雇来的打手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The landlord sent bullyboys to frighten the tenants.  房东派打手去恐吓房客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hector</td><td>/ˈhektər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>威吓，欺凌恫吓</td><td>hector the staff<br>恫吓下属</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach hectored his players throughout the second half.  整个下半场教练都在对球员大声恫吓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intimidator</td><td>/ɪnˈtɪməˌdeɪtər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>威吓他人者，恐吓者</td><td>a notorious intimidator<br>出名的恐吓者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was known as an intimidator who silenced every critic.  他以恐吓者闻名，让所有批评者都不敢发声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 underdog</td><td>/ˈʌndərdɔːɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>弱者，处于劣势的一方</td><td>root for the underdog<br>支持处于劣势者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everyone loves to see the underdog win in the final match.  每个人都喜欢看到弱者在决赛中获胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bumptious</b></td><td>/ˈbʌmpʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>专横傲慢的，自以为是的</td><td>a bumptious young man<br>自以为是的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her bumptious manner annoyed the older members of the committee.  她自以为是的态度让委员会里年长的成员很反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 assumptive</del></td><td>/əˈsʌmptɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，自以为是的；假定的</td><td>an assumptive attitude<br>傲慢的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His assumptive manner annoyed everyone at the negotiating table.  他傲慢的态度让谈判桌上的人都很反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supercilious</td><td>/ˌsuːpərˈsɪliəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>高傲的，傲慢的</td><td>a supercilious smile<br>傲慢的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A supercilious smile crossed his face when he heard my question.  听到我的问题，他脸上掠过傲慢的微笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperious</td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>an imperious tone<br>专横的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her imperious manner made junior colleagues afraid to disagree.  她专横的态度让年轻同事不敢有异议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overweening</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈwiːnɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>overweening pride<br>过度的骄傲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His overweening confidence led him to ignore every warning from the engineers.  他过度的自信使他忽视了工程师的每一次警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peremptory</td><td>/pəˈremptəri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不容置疑的；专横傲慢的</td><td>a peremptory order<br>不容置疑的命令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His peremptory tone left no room for questions or discussion.  他傲慢的语气不给任何提问或讨论的余地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pompous</td><td>/ˈpɑːmpəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的；傲慢的</td><td>a pompous speech<br>浮夸的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pompous speech bored everyone in the crowded town hall.  那场浮夸的演讲让拥挤的市政厅里每个人都厌烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 presuming</td><td>/prɪˈzuːmɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自以为是的，冒失放肆的</td><td>a presuming stranger<br>冒失的陌生人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was presuming of him to offer advice uninvited.  他未经邀请就给建议，实在冒失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 presumptuous</td><td>/prɪˈzʌmptʃuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冒失的，放肆的</td><td>presumptuous of you<br>你太放肆了</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It seems presumptuous to criticize a book you have not read.  批评一本没读过的书，似乎太放肆了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assertive</td><td>/əˈsɜːrtɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自信的</td><td>an assertive negotiator<br>自信果断的谈判者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A more assertive negotiator would have asked for twice the money.  更自信的谈判者会要求双倍的钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 haughty</td><td>/ˈhɔːti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，目中无人的</td><td>a haughty manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave the waiter a haughty look and said nothing.  她傲慢地看了服务员一眼，什么也没说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humble</td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modest</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谦虚的；不大的，适度的</td><td>modest about her success<br>对成功很谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed modest about his award and thanked his team.  他对自己的奖项很谦虚，还感谢了团队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unarrogant</del></td><td>/ʌnˈærəɡənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不傲慢的，谦逊的</td><td>quiet and unarrogant<br>安静而谦逊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Despite his fame he remained a modest, unarrogant teacher.  尽管成名，他仍是一位谦和不傲的老师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unpretentious</td><td>/ˌʌnprɪˈtenʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不炫耀的，朴实低调的</td><td>an unpretentious restaurant<br>朴实的小餐馆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They live in a small, unpretentious house near the river.  他们住在河边一间朴实的小房子里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bungle</b></td><td>/ˈbʌŋɡl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>办糟，失败</td><td>bungle the job<br>把工作办糟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crew bungled the repair and flooded the entire basement.  工人把维修办糟了，整个地下室都被水淹了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 boggle</td><td>/ˈbɑːɡl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>（因为怀疑、恐惧）犹豫</td><td>boggle at the risk<br>因风险而犹豫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investors still boggle at the risk of such a large loan.  投资者仍因如此大额贷款的风险而犹豫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bumble</td><td>/ˈbʌmbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>含糊不清地说，杂乱无章地说</td><td>bumble through a speech<br>含糊地讲完演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He bumbled through his answer and confused the whole audience.  他含糊其辞地答完，把全场听众都弄糊涂了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fumble</td><td>/ˈfʌmbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>笨拙地做</td><td>fumble for the keys<br>笨拙地摸找钥匙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He fumbled for the keys in his dark coat pocket.  他在昏暗的外套口袋里笨拙地摸找钥匙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mess up</td><td>/mɛs ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>弄糟，搞乱</td><td>mess up the plan<br>把计划搞乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not mess up the report the night before the deadline.  别在截止前夜把报告搞砸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 screw up</td><td>/skru ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>办糟，搞砸</td><td>screw up an interview<br>把面试搞砸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He screwed up his first interview but got the job anyway.  他把第一次面试搞砸了，但最后还是拿到了工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bring off</td><td>/brɪŋ ˈɔf/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>成功完成，办成</td><td>bring off a difficult deal<br>促成一笔难做的交易</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Somehow she brought off the rescue without any help.  她不知怎么在无人相助下完成了营救。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>buoy</b></td><td>/ˈbuːi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使充满勇气和力量，使振作</td><td>buoy his spirits<br>使他振作起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Kind letters from strangers buoyed her spirits during the long illness.  陌生人的暖心来信在她久病期间让她振作起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 embolden</td><td>/ɪmˈboʊldən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓励，使大胆</td><td>embolden the rebels<br>壮了叛乱者的胆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Early success emboldened her to ask for a promotion.  初期的成功让她有胆量要求升职。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hearten</td><td>/ˈhɑːrtn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>给予鼓励，鼓舞</td><td>hearten the troops<br>鼓舞部队士气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd's cheering heartened the runners on the last steep hill.  观众的欢呼在最后一个陡坡上鼓舞了跑者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inspire</td><td>/ɪnˈspaɪr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓舞，激励；引发</td><td>inspire confidence in others<br>让他人产生信心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her speech inspired the students to work much harder.  她的演讲激励学生更加努力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bear up</td><td>/bɛr ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>坚持住，不气馁</td><td>bear up under pressure<br>在压力下坚持住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She bore up bravely through the long months of treatment.  在漫长的数月治疗中她勇敢地坚持着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 buck up</td><td>/bʌk ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>振作起来，打起精神</td><td>buck up a discouraged friend<br>让沮丧的朋友振作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A short walk and some coffee bucked him up nicely.  散散步再喝点咖啡，他精神好了很多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 daunt</td><td>/dɔːnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使胆怯，吓倒</td><td>daunt the bravest soldiers<br>吓倒最勇敢的士兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The steep climb ahead did not daunt the experienced mountain guide.  前方陡峭的攀登丝毫没有吓倒这位经验丰富的山地导游。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 discourage</td><td>/dɪsˈkɜːrɪdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使沮丧，使气馁；劝阻</td><td>discourage him from trying<br>劝他不要尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Early failure should not discourage a young researcher.  早期的失败不该让年轻研究者气馁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dishearten</td><td>/dɪsˈhɑːrtn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使沮丧，使失去信心</td><td>disheartened by the news<br>因这消息而沮丧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The string of early losses disheartened even the most loyal fans.  开局连输让最忠实的球迷也失去了信心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 dispirit</del></td><td>/dɪˈspɪrɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使沮丧，使气馁</td><td>dispirit the whole team<br>使全队士气低落</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The endless rain dispirited even the most cheerful campers.  连绵的阴雨让最开朗的营员也沮丧起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>buoyant</b></td><td>/ˈbuːjənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>心情好的</td><td>in a buoyant mood<br>心情愉快</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived in a buoyant mood after hearing the good news.  听到好消息后，他心情愉快地到了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blithe</td><td>/blaɪð/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愉快高兴的；无忧无虑的；漫不经心的</td><td>a blithe disregard<br>漫不经心的无视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His blithe disregard for the rules annoyed the older staff.  他对规则漫不经心的无视让资深员工不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chipper</td><td>/ˈtʃɪpər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>活泼快活的，精神好的</td><td>feel chipper this morning<br>今早心情不错</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sounded surprisingly chipper for someone who slept three hours.  对只睡了三小时的人来说，她听起来出奇地精神。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 eupeptic</del></td><td>/juˈpɛptɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>乐观愉快的；消化良好的</td><td>a eupeptic mood<br>愉快乐观的心情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His eupeptic outlook made even bad news seem manageable.  他乐观的心态让坏消息也显得可以应付。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 lightsome</del></td><td>/ˈlaɪtsəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻快的，快活的</td><td>a lightsome step<br>轻快的步子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She crossed the room with a lightsome, dancing step.  她以轻快如舞的步子穿过房间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 upbeat</td><td>/ˈʌpbiːt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>乐观向上的，令人振奋的</td><td>an upbeat mood<br>乐观的心情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Despite the heavy loss, the coach stayed upbeat about the coming season.  尽管惨败，教练对下个赛季依然乐观。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 winsome</td><td>/ˈwɪnsəm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，漂亮的</td><td>a winsome smile<br>迷人的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her winsome smile made the shy children relax almost at once.  她迷人的微笑让害羞的孩子们几乎立刻放松下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 effervescent</td><td>/ˌefərˈvesənt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情活泼的；起泡的</td><td>an effervescent personality<br>活泼热情的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her effervescent laughter filled the small crowded kitchen.  她欢快的笑声充满了狭小拥挤的厨房。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dour</td><td>/ˈdaʊər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严厉的</td><td>a dour expression<br>严厉的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guard's dour expression discouraged anyone from asking him for directions.  警卫严厉的表情让人不敢向他问路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gloomy</td><td>/ˈɡluːmi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧郁的</td><td>a gloomy mood<br>忧郁的心情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She grew gloomy whenever the winter rain lasted more than a week.  每当冬雨连下一周以上，她就变得忧郁起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 morose</td><td>/məˈroʊs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧郁的</td><td>grow morose and silent<br>变得忧郁沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After losing the election he grew morose and rarely left his apartment.  落选之后他变得忧郁，很少离开公寓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 saturnine</td><td>/ˈsætərnaɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧郁的，阴沉的</td><td>a saturnine temperament<br>阴沉的性情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His saturnine expression discouraged anyone from starting a cheerful conversation.  他阴沉的表情让人不敢开启轻松的话题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sullen</td><td>/ˈsʌlən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>闷闷不乐的</td><td>a sullen silence<br>闷闷不乐的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sullen silence fell over the table when the topic was raised.  提到这个话题时，餐桌上陷入闷闷不乐的沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>burgeon</b></td><td>/ˈbɜːrdʒən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>迅速成长扩大，蓬勃发展</td><td>a burgeoning market<br>迅速发展的市场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city's tech sector burgeoned after the new university opened.  新大学开办后，该市的科技产业迅速壮大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accelerate</td><td>/əkˈseləreɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>加速，加快</td><td>accelerate the process<br>加快进程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New funding accelerated the development of the vaccine.  新的资金加快了疫苗的研发。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accumulate</td><td>/əˈkjuːmjəleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、及物动词</td><td>逐渐增长，积累</td><td>accumulate wealth<br>积累财富</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dust had accumulated on the shelves during our absence.  我们离开期间架子上积了灰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 balloon</td><td>/bəˈluːn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>迅速增加</td><td>costs have ballooned<br>成本迅速膨胀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Repair costs ballooned from two thousand to twenty thousand dollars.  维修费用从两千美元猛增到两万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 boom</td><td>/ˈbuːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>繁荣，激增；轰鸣</td><td>an economic boom<br>经济繁荣期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tourism boomed after the new airport opened last spring.  新机场去年春天启用后，旅游业迅速繁荣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 buildup</td><td>/ˈbɪldʌp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>增长，积聚；大力宣传</td><td>a buildup of pressure<br>压力的不断积聚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors noticed a slow buildup of fluid in his lungs.  医生注意到他肺部液体缓慢积聚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 escalate</td><td>/ˈeskəleɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）（战争等）升级，扩大</td><td>escalate into violence<br>升级为暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The border dispute quickly escalated into a full-scale armed conflict.  边界争端迅速升级为全面武装冲突。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mount</td><td>/ˈmaʊnt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>不及物动词、及物动词</td><td>增长，上升</td><td>tension mounts<br>紧张气氛加剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Pressure on the minister mounted as the scandal spread.  随着丑闻扩散，部长承受的压力不断增加。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 multiply</td><td>/ˈmʌltɪplaɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>大量增加，繁殖；相乘</td><td>multiply rapidly<br>迅速繁殖增多</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Bacteria multiply quickly in warm and damp conditions.  细菌在温暖潮湿的环境中迅速繁殖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mushroom</td><td>/ˈmʌʃruːm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>蘑菇；迅速增长</td><td>mushroom into a crisis<br>迅速演变成危机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Small online shops mushroomed across the country within two years.  小型网店两年内在全国迅速涌现。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proliferate</td><td>/prəˈlɪfəreɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>快速繁殖；激增</td><td>proliferate rapidly online<br>在网上迅速激增</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap smartphones proliferated across the country in just five years.  廉价智能手机在短短五年内遍布全国。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rollup</td><td>/ˈroʊlʌp/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>累积，汇总；卷起之物</td><td>a rollup of costs<br>成本的汇总累加</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report gave a monthly rollup of all regional sales.  报告给出了各区域销售的月度汇总。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snowball</td><td>/ˈsnoʊˌbɔːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>迅速增长，滚雪球般扩大</td><td>snowball into a crisis<br>演变成一场危机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A small complaint snowballed into a national controversy.  一个小小的投诉迅速演变成全国性争议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wax</td><td>/wæks/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>增大，增强</td><td>wax and wane<br>盛衰交替，此消彼长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His influence in the party waxed steadily during the long campaign.  在漫长的竞选中，他在党内的影响力稳步增强。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flourish</td><td>/ˈflɜːrɪʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>茂盛；繁荣</td><td>flourish in warm climates<br>在温暖气候中繁茂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Olive trees flourish in the dry, sunny hills of southern Spain.  橄榄树在西班牙南部干燥向阳的山丘上长得茂盛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prosper</td><td>/ˈprɑːspər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>繁荣，兴旺，成功</td><td>prosper under new management<br>在新管理下兴旺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The small bakery prospered once tourists discovered the town.  游客发现这个小镇后，那家小面包店就兴旺起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wane</td><td>/weɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>减少，衰退</td><td>wax and wane<br>盈亏消长，兴衰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Public interest in the scandal waned within a month.  公众对这桩丑闻的兴趣一个月内就减退了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wither</td><td>/ˈwɪðər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>枯萎，凋谢</td><td>wither in the heat<br>在高温中枯萎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tomato plants withered after a week without rain or shade.  一周无雨又没有遮荫，番茄苗都枯萎了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 waste away</td><td>/weɪst əˈweɪ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>日渐消瘦，逐渐衰弱</td><td>waste away from illness<br>因病日渐消瘦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Without proper food the prisoners slowly wasted away.  没有足够食物，囚犯们渐渐消瘦衰弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 subside</td><td>/səbˈsaɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>减弱，平息；下沉</td><td>the pain finally subsided<br>疼痛终于减轻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The noise subsided as soon as the teacher walked in.  老师一进来，喧闹声就平息了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 subdue</td><td>/səbˈduː/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使顺从；征服</td><td>subdue the rebellion<br>镇压叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Government forces moved south to subdue the rebellion within a week.  政府军南下，一周内镇压了叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>burlesque</b></td><td>/bɜːrˈlesk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>滑稽夸张的模仿讽刺作品；恶搞</td><td>a burlesque of politics<br>对政治的滑稽恶搞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The play is a clever burlesque of modern political debate.  这部戏是对当代政治辩论的巧妙滑稽模仿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 caricature</td><td>/ˈkærɪkətʃər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>讽刺画，夸张的滑稽模仿</td><td>draw a caricature<br>画一幅讽刺画</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cartoonist drew a cruel caricature of the new mayor.  漫画家画了一幅刻薄的新市长讽刺画。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imitate</td><td>/ˈɪməˌteɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>模仿，仿效</td><td>imitate his accent<br>模仿他的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children naturally imitate the speech of adults around them.  孩子会自然地模仿身边大人的说话方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mock</td><td>/ˈmɑːk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>假的，模拟的；嘲笑</td><td>a mock trial<br>模拟审判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students held a mock election to learn how voting works.  学生举行模拟选举以了解投票如何运作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 parody</td><td>/ˈpærədi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（以嘲笑原作作者的）模仿作品，恶搞</td><td>a parody of the film<br>对该片的恶搞模仿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The students wrote a hilarious parody of the morning speech at assembly.  学生们写了一篇滑稽的作品，恶搞集会上的早间讲话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spoof</td><td>/spuːf/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>轻松幽默的模仿，小恶搞</td><td>a spoof of the news<br>对新闻的幽默模仿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The show aired a clever spoof of the evening news broadcast.  节目播出了对晚间新闻的一段巧妙恶搞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 travesty</td><td>/ˈtrævəsti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>拙劣的、嘲弄性模仿</td><td>a travesty of justice<br>对正义的拙劣讽刺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics called the hasty trial a travesty of justice.  批评者称这场匆忙的审判是对正义的拙劣讽刺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 9

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>burnish</b></td><td>/ˈbɜːrnɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>擦亮，磨光</td><td>burnish his reputation<br>为他的名声增光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent the afternoon burnishing the brass handles until they gleamed.  他花了一下午把铜把手擦得发亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 buff</td><td>/bʌf/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>擦亮，磨光；爱好者</td><td>buff the car to shine<br>把车擦得发亮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He buffed the old silver tray until it gleamed.  他把那只旧银盘擦得闪闪发亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 polish</td><td>/ˈpɑːlɪʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>擦亮，磨光；润色</td><td>polish the wooden floor<br>把木地板擦亮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She polished her essay carefully before sending it in.  她仔细润色文章后才交上去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 furbish</del></td><td>/ˈfɜːrbɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>磨光，擦亮；翻新</td><td>furbish an old house<br>翻新一栋旧房子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They furbished the old theater before the festival began.  他们在节庆开始前翻新了那家老剧院。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grind</td><td>/ˈɡraɪnd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>磨碎，磨光；苦干</td><td>grind the lens smooth<br>把镜片磨光滑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers ground the marble surface until it shone.  工人把大理石表面磨到发亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smoothen</td><td>/ˈsmuːðən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使光滑，把…弄平</td><td>smoothen the rough edges<br>把粗糙的边缘弄平</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A little sanding will smoothen the surface of the board.  稍加打磨就能把木板表面弄平滑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>buttress</b></td><td>/ˈbʌtrɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支持，加强（论点）</td><td>buttress an argument<br>为论点提供支撑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fresh evidence buttressed the prosecutor's case against the two suspects.  新证据支持了检察官针对两名嫌疑人的指控。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 back</td><td>/ˈbæk/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支持，资助</td><td>back a candidate<br>支持某位候选人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several investors agreed to back her new company.  几位投资人同意资助她的新公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bolster</td><td>/ˈboʊlstər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支持，增强</td><td>bolster his confidence<br>增强他的信心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A word of praise from her would bolster his confidence enormously.  她一句赞扬就会极大地增强他的信心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 corroborate</td><td>/kəˈrɑːbəreɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>用证据或权威证实；为…提供证据，支持</td><td>corroborate the witness's story<br>证实证人的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two neighbors corroborated the driver's account of the accident that night.  两位邻居证实了司机对那晚事故的说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reinforce</td><td>/ˌriːɪnˈfɔːrs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加强，强化</td><td>reinforce the message<br>强化这一信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Repeated praise reinforced the child's interest in reading.  反复的表扬强化了孩子对阅读的兴趣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 substantiate</td><td>/səbˈstænʃieɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证实</td><td>substantiate the claim<br>证实这一说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No evidence has emerged to substantiate his serious accusations.  没有证据能证实他严重的指控。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shore up</td><td>/ʃɔr ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>支撑，加固；支持</td><td>shore up public support<br>巩固公众支持</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The government acted quickly to shore up the failing banks.  政府迅速采取行动支撑濒临倒闭的银行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 contravene</td><td>/ˌkɑːntrəˈviːn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>违反，反对</td><td>contravene the rules<br>违反规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Building a factory here would contravene several local environmental regulations.  在此建厂会违反数条当地环保法规。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 challenge</td><td>/ˈtʃæləndʒ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>质疑，对…提出挑战</td><td>challenge the theory<br>质疑该理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several scientists challenged the conclusions of the new study.  几位科学家质疑这项新研究的结论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>byzantine</b></td><td>/ˈbɪzəntiːn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>错综复杂的</td><td>byzantine bureaucracy<br>错综复杂的官僚体制</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tax code is so byzantine that even experts disagree.  税法错综复杂，连专家都各执一词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 convoluted</td><td>/ˈkɑːnvəluːtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>复杂的，费解的</td><td>a convoluted argument<br>费解的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The instructions were so convoluted that nobody could assemble the shelf.  说明书太过费解，没人能装好那个架子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intricate</td><td>/ˈɪntrəkət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>错综复杂的，精细的</td><td>an intricate pattern<br>精细的图案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The carpet shows an intricate pattern of leaves and birds.  地毯上是精细复杂的叶与鸟图案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 involved</td><td>/ɪnˈvɑːlvd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>错综复杂的，难懂的</td><td>an involved argument<br>错综复杂的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plot is so involved that few readers finish the book.  情节太过错综复杂，很少有读者读完这本书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 labyrinthine</td><td>/ˌlæbəˈrɪnθaɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷宫似的，复杂曲折的</td><td>labyrinthine corridors<br>迷宫般的走廊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We got lost in the labyrinthine corridors of the old hospital building.  我们在这座老医院大楼迷宫般的走廊里迷了路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sophisticated</td><td>/səˈfɪstɪkeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>复杂精密的；老练的</td><td>a sophisticated system<br>复杂精密的系统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lab uses highly sophisticated equipment to detect the particles.  实验室用极为精密的设备探测这些粒子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tangled</td><td>/ˈtæŋɡəld/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>纠缠的，错综复杂的</td><td>a tangled web<br>错综复杂的关系网</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The case involves a tangled web of loans and false names.  此案涉及贷款与假名交织的复杂网络。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 straightforward</td><td>/ˌstreɪtˈfɔːrwərd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简单明了的；坦率的</td><td>a straightforward procedure<br>简单直接的流程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The instructions are straightforward enough for a child to follow.  说明简单明了，连孩子都能照着做。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plain</td><td>/ˈpleɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>完全的，十足的；朴素的，清楚的</td><td>plain stupid<br>简直愚蠢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ignoring the warning was just plain stupid.  忽视警告纯粹是愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 simple</td><td>/ˈsɪmpəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简单的，容易的</td><td>a simple task<br>简单的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Follow these simple steps and the machine will restart.  按这些简单步骤操作，机器就会重启。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uncomplicated</td><td>/ˌʌnˈkɑːmplɪkeɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不复杂的，简单的</td><td>an uncomplicated design<br>不复杂的设计</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He preferred an uncomplicated life in a quiet mountain village.  他偏爱在宁静山村过简单的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cache</b></td><td>/kæʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>囤货，藏货</td><td>a cache of weapons<br>一批藏匿的武器</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police discovered a cache of weapons beneath the barn floor.  警方在谷仓地板下发现了一批藏匿的武器。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stash</td><td>/ˈstæʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>藏匿，私藏；藏匿物</td><td>stash the money away<br>把钱藏起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She stashed the letters in a box under her bed.  她把那些信藏在床下的盒子里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stockpile</td><td>/ˈstɑːkˌpaɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>储备，大量囤积</td><td>a stockpile of grain<br>储备的大批粮食</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The country stockpiled fuel before the long winter arrived.  这个国家在漫长冬季到来前囤积燃料。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 store</td><td>/ˈstɔːr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>储存，贮藏；储备物</td><td>store food for winter<br>为冬天储存食物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Squirrels store nuts in hollow trees every autumn.  松鼠每年秋天把坚果藏在树洞里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deposit</td><td>/dɪˈpɑːzɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>自然积累，沉积，矿藏；存钱</td><td>a deposit of coal<br>一处煤的矿藏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Geologists found a rich deposit of copper beneath the dry valley.  地质学家在干谷下面发现了丰富的铜矿藏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hoard</td><td>/hɔːrd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>贮藏，秘藏</td><td>hoard food and water<br>贮藏食物和水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Squirrels hoard nuts in hollow trees long before the first frost.  松鼠早在初霜之前就把坚果贮藏在树洞里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reserve</td><td>/rɪˈzɜːrv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>储备，保留；预订</td><td>a reserve of cash<br>一笔现金储备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city keeps a reserve of water for dry summers.  该市为干旱的夏季保留一份储水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cachet</b></td><td>/kæˈʃeɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>声望，威望；正式批准</td><td>carry a certain cachet<br>带有某种威望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A degree from that school still carries considerable cachet here.  那所学校的学位在这里仍然很有声望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 approval</td><td>/əˈpruːvəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>批准，认可；赞成</td><td>seek her approval<br>征求她的同意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plan needs approval from the board before work begins.  该计划开工前需要董事会批准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 authorization</td><td>/ˌɔːθərəˈzeɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>授权，批准</td><td>official authorization<br>正式授权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one may enter the lab without written authorization.  未经书面授权，任何人不得进入实验室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 concurrence</td><td>/kənˈkɜːrəns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>同意，一致；同时发生</td><td>with the concurrence of<br>在…同意之下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plan proceeded with the concurrence of all board members.  该计划在全体董事同意之下推进。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 endorsement</td><td>/ɪnˈdɔːrsmənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>认可，赞同；代言</td><td>a public endorsement<br>公开的支持认可</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The candidate won the endorsement of two major unions.  这位候选人获得两大工会的支持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 license</td><td>/ˈlaɪsns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>许可，认可；自由</td><td>a license to practice<br>执业许可证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She finally obtained a license to practice medicine in three different states.  她终于取得了在三个不同州行医的执业许可。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 permission</td><td>/pərˈmɪʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>许可，允许</td><td>ask for permission<br>请求许可</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 You need permission from the owner before entering the field.  进入这片地之前需要得到主人许可。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sanction</td><td>/ˈsæŋkʃn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>批准，同意，认可</td><td>sanction the new policy<br>批准这项新政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board refused to sanction the plan without a detailed budget.  董事会拒绝在没有详细预算的情况下批准该计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 warrant</td><td>/ˈwɔːrənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>保证，担保；使有正当理由</td><td>warrant the quality<br>保证质量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The factory warrants the quality of every tool it sells abroad.  这家工厂为其出口的每件工具的质量提供保证。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 credit</td><td>/ˈkredɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>表扬，赞扬；信任</td><td>take credit for<br>把功劳归于自己</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She deserves credit for keeping the project alive.  她值得称赞，因为她让这个项目坚持下来了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distinction</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋkʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>殊荣，卓越；区别</td><td>a mark of distinction<br>卓越的标志</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He graduated with distinction from a small rural college.  他以优异成绩从一所乡下的小型学院毕业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 esteem</td><td>/ɪˈstiːm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>尊重，敬重</td><td>hold in high esteem<br>十分敬重</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her colleagues esteem her for her honesty and courage.  同事们因她的诚实与勇气而敬重她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 homage</td><td>/ˈhɑːmɪdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>尊敬，敬意</td><td>pay homage to<br>向…致敬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thousands came to pay homage to the poet on her ninetieth birthday.  数千人在诗人九十岁生日那天前来致敬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 kudos</td><td>/ˈkuːdoʊz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>名望，名声；夸奖，赞扬</td><td>kudos for the win<br>因获胜而受赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She earned kudos from critics for her fearless debut novel.  她凭无畏的处女作小说赢得了评论界的赞誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prestige</td><td>/preˈstiːʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>声望，威望；有威望的</td><td>enjoy great prestige<br>享有很高声望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old university still enjoys enormous prestige among employers worldwide.  这所老牌大学在全球雇主中仍享有极高声望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 interdiction</td><td>/ˌɪntərˈdɪkʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>禁止，阻断，封锁</td><td>drug interdiction efforts<br>禁毒阻断行动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Naval interdiction stopped most of the smuggling along the coast.  海军的阻断行动制止了沿岸大部分走私。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prohibition</td><td>/ˌproʊəˈbɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>禁止，禁令</td><td>a prohibition on smoking<br>禁止吸烟的规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city enforced a prohibition against building near the lake.  该市执行了禁止在湖边建房的规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 proscription</td><td>/proʊˈskrɪpʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>禁止，查禁；剥夺权利</td><td>the proscription of books<br>对书籍的查禁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wartime proscription of certain newspapers lasted for several years.  战时对某些报纸的查禁持续了数年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 infamy</td><td>/ˈɪnfəmi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恶名，臭名；恶行</td><td>a day of infamy<br>耻辱的一天</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His name lives on in infamy after the scandal.  丑闻之后他的名字以恶名流传下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 notoriety</td><td>/ˌnoʊtəˈraɪəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>坏名声</td><td>gain instant notoriety<br>立刻声名狼藉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The video gained him instant notoriety that followed him for the rest of his career.  这段视频让他立刻声名狼藉，并伴随他整个职业生涯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cacophony</b></td><td>/kəˈkɑːfəni/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>刺耳的声音</td><td>a cacophony of horns<br>刺耳的喇叭声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A cacophony of horns filled the street during rush hour.  高峰时段街上充满刺耳的喇叭声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blare</td><td>/ˈbler/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>响亮刺耳的声音；轰响</td><td>the blare of trumpets<br>响亮刺耳的号声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Music blared from the open windows of the passing car.  音乐从驶过汽车敞开的车窗里轰响出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bluster</td><td>/ˈblʌstər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>狂妄自大地大声说；喧闹的状态</td><td>bluster and threaten<br>大声吓唬威胁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager began to bluster and threaten when questioned closely.  被仔细追问时，经理开始狂妄自大地大声吓唬威胁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clamor</td><td>/ˈklæmər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>喧闹，喧哗，噪声</td><td>a clamor of voices<br>一片人声喧哗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A clamor of voices rose from the crowded courtyard.  拥挤的院子里传出一片人声喧哗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 din</td><td>/ˈdɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>嘈杂喧闹的声音</td><td>the din of traffic<br>交通的喧闹声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We could barely talk above the din of the factory.  在工厂的喧闹声中我们几乎无法交谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discordance</td><td>/dɪsˈkɔːrdəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不一致，不和</td><td>discordance between reports<br>各报告之间的矛盾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a clear discordance between the two witnesses' accounts.  两位证人的叙述之间存在明显不一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 racket</td><td>/ˈrækɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>喧闹吵嚷声；球拍</td><td>make a terrible racket<br>发出可怕的喧闹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children upstairs made a racket all afternoon long.  楼上的孩子整个下午都在吵闹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quiet</td><td>/ˈkwaɪət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>安静的；平静的</td><td>keep the room quiet<br>保持房间安静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library stays quiet even during exam week.  即使在考试周，图书馆也保持安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 silence</td><td>/ˈsaɪləns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>寂静，沉默；使沉默</td><td>break the silence<br>打破沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A long silence followed his surprising announcement to the class.  他向全班宣布这惊人消息后是长久的沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 still</td><td>/ˈstɪl/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>寂静无声的；寂静</td><td>the still of night<br>夜晚的寂静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The air was still and the lake looked like glass.  空气静止无风，湖面像玻璃一样。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stillness</td><td>/ˈstɪlnəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>寂静，静止</td><td>the stillness of dawn<br>黎明时的寂静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a distant bell broke the stillness of the valley.  只有远处的钟声打破了山谷的寂静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cavalier</b></td><td>/ˌkævəˈlɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>有骑士风度的人；傲慢轻率的</td><td>a cavalier attitude<br>傲慢轻率的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company took a cavalier attitude toward worker safety.  这家公司对工人安全的态度傲慢轻率。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chevalier</td><td>/ˌʃevəˈlɪr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骑士；有骑士风度的人</td><td>a French chevalier<br>一位法国骑士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old chevalier still bowed like a courtier of Versailles.  这位老骑士仍像凡尔赛宫的朝臣那样行礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arrogant</td><td>/ˈærəɡənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，自大的</td><td>an arrogant manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His arrogant remarks cost him the respect of his colleagues.  他傲慢的言论使他失去了同事的尊重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 assumptive</del></td><td>/əˈsʌmptɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，自以为是的；假定的</td><td>an assumptive attitude<br>傲慢的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His assumptive manner annoyed everyone at the negotiating table.  他傲慢的态度让谈判桌上的人都很反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bumptious</td><td>/ˈbʌmpʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>专横傲慢的，自以为是的</td><td>a bumptious young man<br>自以为是的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her bumptious manner annoyed the older members of the committee.  她自以为是的态度让委员会里年长的成员很反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 haughty</td><td>/ˈhɔːti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，目中无人的</td><td>a haughty manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave the waiter a haughty look and said nothing.  她傲慢地看了服务员一眼，什么也没说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperious</td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>an imperious tone<br>专横的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her imperious manner made junior colleagues afraid to disagree.  她专横的态度让年轻同事不敢有异议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lofty</td><td>/ˈlɔːfti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>崇高的；自大的</td><td>a lofty tone<br>自大的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dismissed our idea with a lofty wave of her hand.  她自大地一挥手就否定了我们的想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overweening</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈwiːnɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>overweening pride<br>过度的骄傲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His overweening confidence led him to ignore every warning from the engineers.  他过度的自信使他忽视了工程师的每一次警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peremptory</td><td>/pəˈremptəri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不容置疑的；专横傲慢的</td><td>a peremptory order<br>不容置疑的命令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His peremptory tone left no room for questions or discussion.  他傲慢的语气不给任何提问或讨论的余地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pompous</td><td>/ˈpɑːmpəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的；傲慢的</td><td>a pompous speech<br>浮夸的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pompous speech bored everyone in the crowded town hall.  那场浮夸的演讲让拥挤的市政厅里每个人都厌烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sumptuous</td><td>/ˈsʌmptʃuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>豪华的，奢侈的</td><td>a sumptuous banquet<br>一场豪华的宴会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hotel served a sumptuous banquet for the visiting delegation.  酒店为来访代表团摆了一场奢华的宴会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supercilious</td><td>/ˌsuːpərˈsɪliəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>高傲的，傲慢的</td><td>a supercilious smile<br>傲慢的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A supercilious smile crossed his face when he heard my question.  听到我的问题，他脸上掠过傲慢的微笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 culprit</td><td>/ˈkʌlprɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>罪犯，肇事者；起因</td><td>find the real culprit<br>找出真正的肇事者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police soon identified the culprit behind the string of thefts.  警方很快查明这一连串盗窃的肇事者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 devil</td><td>/ˈdevəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>魔鬼，恶魔；坏家伙</td><td>a poor devil<br>可怜的家伙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In the old story the devil offers wealth for a soul.  在那个古老故事里，恶魔用财富换取灵魂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 felon</td><td>/ˈfelən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>重罪犯</td><td>a convicted felon<br>已定罪的重罪犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The law bars a convicted felon from owning any firearm.  法律禁止已定罪的重罪犯持有枪械。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fiend</td><td>/ˈfiːnd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>恶魔，残忍之人；狂热者</td><td>a cruel fiend<br>残忍的恶魔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The newspapers described the killer as a heartless fiend.  报纸把这个凶手描述成毫无心肠的恶魔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 miscreant</td><td>/ˈmɪskriənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>恶棍，罪犯；邪恶的</td><td>punish the miscreants<br>惩罚这些恶棍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge promised to punish the miscreants who had vandalized the village school.  法官承诺惩罚那些破坏村校的恶棍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rapscallion</td><td>/ræpˈskæliən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>流氓，无赖；小捣蛋鬼</td><td>a young rapscallion<br>一个年轻流氓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That little rapscallion stole apples from the neighbor's tree again.  那个小捣蛋鬼又偷了邻居树上的苹果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rascal</td><td>/ˈræskəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>无赖，流氓；小淘气</td><td>a little rascal<br>小淘气鬼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That rascal took the last piece of cake again.  那个小淘气又拿走了最后一块蛋糕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reprobate</td><td>/ˈreprəbeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>堕落者，道德败坏的人</td><td>an old reprobate<br>一个老无赖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel's hero is a charming reprobate with no money.  小说的主角是个迷人却身无分文的堕落者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 villain</td><td>/ˈvɪlən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>恶棍；反派人物</td><td>the villain of the story<br>故事里的反派</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every good film needs a villain the audience loves hating.  每部好片都需要一个让观众爱恨的反派。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humble</td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modest</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谦虚的；不大的，适度的</td><td>modest about her success<br>对成功很谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed modest about his award and thanked his team.  他对自己的奖项很谦虚，还感谢了团队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cajole</b></td><td>/kəˈdʒoʊl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>哄骗</td><td>cajole him into agreeing<br>哄他答应下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She cajoled her brother into driving her to the airport.  她哄着哥哥开车送她去机场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 blandish</del></td><td>/ˈblændɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>奉承，讨好，哄诱</td><td>blandish the boss<br>讨好奉承老板</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He blandished the officials until they signed the permit.  他一路奉承官员，直到他们签下许可。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blarney</td><td>/ˈblɑrni/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>花言巧语，奉承话</td><td>full of blarney<br>满口花言巧语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A little blarney got him a free room at the inn.  几句花言巧语让他在旅店白住了一间房。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 palaver</td><td>/pəˈlævər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>闲谈，废话；花言巧语</td><td>endless palaver about money<br>没完没了地谈钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After an hour of pointless palaver, the committee had decided nothing.  经过一小时无意义的闲谈，委员会什么也没决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wheedle</td><td>/ˈwiːdl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>（用花言巧语）诱惑，哄骗</td><td>wheedle money out of him<br>哄他掏出钱来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wheedled her father into lending her the old car.  她哄着父亲把那辆旧车借给她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 soft-soap</td><td>/ˈsɔːft ˌsoʊp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>奉承，讨好</td><td>soft-soap the boss<br>奉承老板</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to soft-soap the inspector into overlooking the fault.  他试图奉承检查员，让他放过这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sweet-talk</td><td>/ˈswit tɔk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>花言巧语地哄骗</td><td>sweet-talk the guard<br>花言巧语哄骗警卫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sweet-talked the clerk into giving him a discount.  他甜言蜜语哄得店员给他打了折。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>calcify</b></del></td><td>/ˈkælsɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使钙化；使僵化固定</td><td>calcify into rigid rules<br>僵化成死板规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Over decades the informal custom calcified into an unbreakable rule.  几十年间，这个非正式习俗僵化成了不可打破的规则。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harden</td><td>/ˈhɑːrdən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使变硬；使变冷酷坚强</td><td>harden into stone<br>变硬得像石头</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of war had hardened the young soldier's heart.  多年战争使这名年轻士兵的心变得冷硬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 ossify</del></td><td>/ˈɑːsɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）硬化，（使）僵化</td><td>ossify into dogma<br>僵化为教条</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Over the decades the bold vision began to ossify into rigid dogma.  几十年间，那个大胆的愿景开始僵化为死板的教条。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 make malleable</td><td>/meɪk ˈmæliəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使有延展性，使易塑</td><td>make malleable by heating<br>加热使之易塑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Careful heating can make even the hardest steel alloy malleable.  仔细加热能让最硬的钢合金也变得易塑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 make pliant</td><td>/meɪk ˈplaɪənt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使柔韧，使易弯曲</td><td>make pliant with steam<br>用蒸汽使之柔韧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Steam is used to make the wood for violin backs pliant.  蒸汽被用来让做琴背的木料变柔韧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 make more flexible</td><td>/meɪk mɔr ˈflɛksəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使更灵活，使更有弹性</td><td>make more flexible rules<br>让规则更灵活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Daily stretching will make the muscles of your back more flexible.  每天拉伸能让你的背部肌肉更灵活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>calibrate</b></td><td>/ˈkælɪbreɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>校准，标定</td><td>calibrate the instrument<br>校准这台仪器</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Technicians calibrate the scales every morning before the lab opens.  技术员每天早上在实验室开门前校准天平。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unstandardize</del></td><td>/ʌnˈstændərdaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使不标准化，取消标准</td><td>unstandardize the test<br>使测试不再标准化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Local schools tried to unstandardize the yearly reading exam.  当地学校试图取消年度阅读考试的标准化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 10

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>calligraphy</b></td><td>/kəˈlɪɡrəfi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>（优美的）书法</td><td>practice Chinese calligraphy<br>练习中国书法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He practices Chinese calligraphy for an hour every evening.  他每天晚上练一小时中国书法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 longhand</td><td>/ˈlɔːŋˌhænd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>普通手写体（非速记）</td><td>write it in longhand<br>用手写体写下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wrote the whole letter in careful longhand.  她用工整的手写体写完了整封信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 manuscript</td><td>/ˈmænjəskrɪpt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>手稿</td><td>an ancient manuscript<br>一份古代手稿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library keeps a medieval manuscript under tightly controlled lighting.  图书馆将一份中世纪手稿保存在严格控制的灯光下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 penmanship</td><td>/ˈpɛnmənʃɪp/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>书法，书写技艺</td><td>neat penmanship<br>工整的书写</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His penmanship was so neat that teachers displayed his notebooks.  他的书写工整得让老师把他的本子展示出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 script</td><td>/skrɪpt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>手写体，字体；剧本</td><td>in elegant script<br>以优雅的字体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The invitation was written in flowing italic script.  邀请函以流畅的斜体字写成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>callous</b></td><td>/ˈkæləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无同情心的，冷漠的</td><td>a callous remark<br>冷漠无情的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His callous joke about the accident shocked everyone in the room.  他拿这场事故开的冷酷玩笑震惊了房间里的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 affectless</del></td><td>/ˈæfektləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无情感的，冷漠的</td><td>an affectless voice<br>毫无感情的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness described the crime in a flat, affectless tone.  证人用平淡无情感的语调描述这桩罪行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uncharitable</td><td>/ʌnˈtʃɛrɪtəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不宽厚的，苛刻无情的</td><td>an uncharitable judgment<br>苛刻无情的评判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be uncharitable to blame her for the delay.  为这次延误责怪她就太苛刻了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unsparing</td><td>/ʌnˈsperɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫不留情的；毫不吝惜的</td><td>unsparing criticism<br>毫不留情的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The review was unsparing about the actor's weak performance.  这篇评论对该演员糟糕的表演毫不留情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remorseless</td><td>/rɪˈmɔːrsləs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫无悔意的，冷酷无情的</td><td>remorseless cruelty<br>冷酷无情的残忍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The killer remained remorseless throughout the long public trial.  凶手在漫长的公开审判中始终毫无悔意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 indurate</del></td><td>/ˈɪndjʊreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使变硬；使冷酷无情</td><td>indurate the heart<br>使心变得冷硬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of betrayal had indurated his heart against every plea for sympathy.  多年的背叛使他的心变得冷硬，对任何求情都无动于衷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ruthless</td><td>/ˈruːθləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有怜悯心的，残忍的</td><td>a ruthless dictator<br>残忍无情的独裁者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general was ruthless with anyone who questioned his orders.  将军对任何质疑其命令的人都残忍无情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sympathetic</td><td>/ˌsɪmpəˈθetɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>同情的，有同情心的</td><td>a sympathetic listener<br>富有同情心的倾听者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse gave her a sympathetic look and held her hand.  护士同情地看着她，握住了她的手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 compassionate</td><td>/kəmˈpæʃənət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有同情心的，慈悲的</td><td>compassionate care<br>充满关怀的照护</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A compassionate nurse sat with the frightened child all night.  一位有同情心的护士整夜陪着那个受惊的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 merciful</td><td>/ˈmɜːrsɪfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>仁慈的，宽厚的</td><td>a merciful judge<br>宽仁的法官</td></tr>
